Hausdorff space
if, given any distinct
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}
, we can find disjoint open subsets
V_{1},V_{2}
such that
z_{1}\in V_{1}
,
z_{2}\in V_{2}
. The space
\mathcal{Z}
is
locally compact
if every point in
\mathcal{Z}
has a compact neighbourhood, i.e. if for every
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, we can find an open set
U\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
and a compact set
K\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
such that
z\in U\subseteq K
.

The main content of the proof of Theorem
1
is Proposition
16
below. Observe that the restriction of the bijection
\psi
in Section
A
to the set
\{(f_{S}:S\subseteq[d]):f_{S}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{S})\;\forall S\subseteq[d]\}
has image
C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
. This identifies
C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
with
\bigl(C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{S}):S\in 2^{[d]}\bigr)
, but henceforth we will not be explicit about this identification, and will simply write
f=(f_{S}:S\in 2^{[d]})\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
. Given such an
f=(f_{S}:S\in 2^{[d]})\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
, we can express the function
f_{\max}
from Section
2.3
as
f_{\max}(x)\coloneqq\max_{S\in 2^{[d]}}f_{S}(x_{S})
for
x\in\mathcal{X}
.

Proposition 16
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{d}
be locally compact Hausdorff spaces, and let
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\prod_{j=1}^{d}\mathcal{X}_{j}
. Assume that every open set in
\mathcal{X}
is
\sigma
-compact. If
P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X})
and
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
, then
Q\in\mathsf{MNAR}_{P}
if and only if

P(f_{\max})\geq Q(f)

for all
f\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}:C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})^{*}
before Lemma
31
. We endow
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})
with the weak topology generated by
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}\bigl(C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})\bigr)
, for
\mathcal{Z}\in\{\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{\star},\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}\}
. This ensures that
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}(g)
is weakly continuous for every
g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})
.

Let
h:\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}\to\mathcal{X}_{\star}
be the continuous function defined by
h(x,S)\coloneqq x\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\bm{1}_{S}
. Then
h
induces a linear map
h_{*}:\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
given by
h_{*}(\mu)(B)\coloneqq\mu\bigl(h^{-1}(B)\bigr)
(see Figure
5
below).
Similarly, let
j:\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}\to\mathcal{X}
be the projection map
j(x,S)\coloneqq x
, and define its induced map
j_{*}:\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
.
We have

\{g\circ h:g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\}\subseteq C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})
and similarly
\{g\circ j:g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X})\}\subseteq C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})
, we have by
Schaefer 1971
that both
h_{*}
and
j_{*}
are weakly continuous. By Lemma
33
, the linear map
T=(h_{*},j_{*}):\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
is continuous when we endow the image space with the product topology, which by Lemma
32
is the same as the weak topology on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
generated by
\phi_{\mathcal{X}_{\star}}\bigl(C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\bigr)\times\phi_{\mathcal{X}}\bigl(C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X})\bigr)
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\star}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\circ h\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}}(g\circ h)\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{X}_{\star}}(g)\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})^{*}}

Figure 5:
Schematic diagrams of various maps defined in the proof. The fact that the maps in the right panel commute follows from the fact that
h_{*}(\mu)(g)=\mu(g\circ h)
for all
g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
.

Define
K
to be the convex cone
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})
. We claim that
h_{*}(K)=\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
. It is clear that
h_{*}(K)\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
since for any
\mu\in K
and any
g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
such that
g\geq 0
, we have by
Folland 1999
that
h_{*}(\mu)(g)=\mu(g\circ h)\geq 0
. For the surjectivity, define
i:\mathcal{X}_{\star}\to\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]}
by
i(z)\coloneqq(z\odot\bm{1}_{\{j:z_{j}\neq\star\}},\{j:z_{j}\neq\star\})
and let
i_{*}:\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}\times 2^{[d]})
be its induced linear map. By the same argument as above, we have
i_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}_{\star}))\subseteq K
. For
\nu
on
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
, we have
\nu=h_{*}\bigl(i_{*}(\nu)\bigr)
, and the surjectivity is established since
i_{*}(\nu)\in K
. Consequently,

\displaystyle h_{*}(K)=\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})=\bigcap_{g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star}):g\geq 0}\{\nu\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star}):\nu(g)\geq 0\}=\bigcap_{g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star}):g\geq 0}\bigl(\phi_{\mathcal{X}_{\star}}(g)\bigr)^{-1}\bigl([0,\infty)\bigr)

is a weakly closed set. A similar argument shows that
j_{*}(K)=\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{X})
is a weakly closed set. Thus,
T(K)
is weakly closed set in
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
, by Lemma
32
.

By definition,
Q\in\mathsf{MNAR}_{P}
if and only if there exists
\mu_{0}\in K
such that
T(\mu_{0})=(Q,P)
. Therefore, by Lemma
31
, we can apply the generalised Farkas’ lemma (Lemma
15
) to obtain that

\displaystyle Q\in\mathsf{MNAR}_{P}

\displaystyle\iff\bigcap_{\mu\in K}\bigl\{(f,g)\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}):h_{*}(\mu)(f)+j_{*}(\mu)(g)\geq 0\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\subseteq\bigl\{(f,g)\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}):Q(f)+P(g)\geq 0\bigr\}.

Now, for any
(f,g)\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})\times C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mu\in K
, we have

h_{*}(\mu)(f)+j_{*}(\mu)(g)=\sum_{S\in 2^{[d]}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\{(f\circ h)(x,S)+g(x)\}\,d\mu(x,S).

Hence,
(f,g)
satisfies
h_{*}(\mu)(f)+j_{*}(\mu)(g)\geq 0
for all
\mu\in K
if and only if
(f\circ h)(x,S)+g(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
S\in 2^{[d]}
. Since
P(g)
is increasing in
g
, it therefore suffices to check that for each
f\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
the function
g_{f}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X})
given by
g_{f}(x)\coloneqq-\min_{S\in 2^{[d]}}(f\circ h)(x,S)=-\min_{S\in 2^{[d]}}f_{S}(x_{S})
satisfies
Q(f)+P(g_{f})\geq 0
. Substituting
f^{\prime}\coloneqq-f
, we have

\displaystyle Q\in\mathsf{MNAR}_{P}

\displaystyle\iff\text{$Q(f)+P(g_{f})\geq 0$ for all $f\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})$}

\displaystyle\iff\text{$Q(f^{\prime})\leq P(f^{\prime}_{\max})$ for all $f^{\prime}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})$}

as desired.
∎

The proof of Theorem
1
now follows in a straightforward fashion.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

From the definition,
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,\pi)
if and only if
Q\in\mathsf{MNAR}_{P}
, which by Proposition
16
occurs if and only if
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
and
P(f_{\max})\geq Q(f)
for all
f\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{\star})
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Suppose that
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
and let
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
be such that
\mu_{\star}(A)=0
. Recall that if
X\sim P
,
B\sim\mathsf{Bern}(\epsilon)
,
\Omega^{(1)}\sim\mathsf{Bern}(q)
and
\Omega^{(2)}\sim\mathsf{Bern}(q_{2})
for some
q_{2}\in[0,1]
with
B\perp\!\!\!\perp(X,\Omega^{(1)},\Omega^{(2)})
and
\Omega^{(1)}\perp\!\!\!\perp X
, then we can generate
Z\sim R
via
Z\coloneqq(1-B)\cdot(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(1)})+B\cdot(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(2)})
. Then by definition of
\mu_{\star}
, we must have
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mu(A)=0
. Since
P\ll\mu
, it follows that

\displaystyle 0=P(A)=\mathbb{P}(X\in A)\geq\mathbb{P}(Z\in A)=R(A).

This proves that
R\ll\mu_{\star}
. Now define
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
by
m(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(2)}=1\,|\,X=x)
. Then for any
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Z\in A)

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon)\cdot\mathbb{P}(X\in A,\,\Omega^{(1)}=1)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{P}(X\in A,\,\Omega^{(2)}=1)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\int_{A}p(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu(x)+\epsilon\cdot\int_{A}m(x)p(x)\;\mathrm{d}\mu(x).

Hence,
\frac{\mathrm{d}R}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(x)=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot p(x)+\epsilon\cdot m(x)p(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\frac{\mathrm{d}R}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(\star)=\mathbb{P}(Z=\star)=1-q(1-\epsilon)-\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)
.

Conversely, suppose that
R\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
satisfies
R\ll\mu_{\star}
, and there exists a Borel measurable function
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
such that
\mathrm{d}R/\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}
satisfies (
63
). Given
X\sim P
, define a random variable
\Omega^{(2)}
taking values in
\{0,1\}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(2)}=1\,|\,X=x)=m(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
B\sim\mathsf{Bern}(\epsilon)
and
\Omega^{(1)}\sim\mathsf{Bern}(q)
be such that
B\perp\!\!\!\perp(X,\Omega^{(1)},\Omega^{(2)})
and
\Omega^{(1)}\perp\!\!\!\perp X
. Then
Z\coloneqq(1-B)\cdot(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(1)})+B\cdot(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(2)})\sim R
and hence by construction
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
.

This completes the proof, but we also provide an alternative proof of the converse statement using Theorem
1
.
Again suppose that
R\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
satisfies
R\ll\mu_{\star}
, and that
\mathrm{d}R/\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}
satisfies (
63
). Define
Q\coloneqq\epsilon^{-1}\{R-(1-\epsilon)\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P)}\}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
as in Theorem
1
, and let
f=(f_{\{1\}},f_{\emptyset})\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
. Note that by definition,
f_{\emptyset}\in\mathbb{R}
is a constant and
f_{\max}(x)=f_{\{1\}}(x)\vee f_{\emptyset}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, since
\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P)}\in\mathcal{R}(P,0,\pi)
, we have by the argument in the direct part of the proof that
\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P)}\ll\mu_{\star}
with
\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P)}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(x)=q\cdot p(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P)}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(\star)=1-q
, so

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(z)=\begin{cases}m(z)p(z)\quad&\text{if }z\in\mathbb{R}\\
1-\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)&\text{if }z=\star.\end{cases}

Hence
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
, and

\displaystyle P(f_{\max})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(f_{\{1\}}(x)\vee f_{\emptyset}\bigr)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{m(x)f_{\{1\}}(x)+\bigl(1-m(x)\bigr)f_{\emptyset}\bigr\}p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=Q(f),

where the inequality follows from the fact that
\max(a,b)
is at least as large as any convex combination of
a
and
b
, for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. We conclude that
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
, by Theorem
1
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs from Section
3

C.1
Proof of Theorem
3

We begin with some lemmas. Recalling the way that we can generate
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}P\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{arb}}\bigl(\theta_{0},\Sigma,\epsilon,\pi\bigr)
from Section
2.2.2
, we let
\mathcal{I}_{n}\subseteq[n]
denote the ‘inliers’, or the indices of the uncontaminated observations. Likewise, we denote by
\mathcal{O}_{n}\subseteq[n]
the ‘outliers’, or the indices of the contaminated observations so that
\mathcal{I}_{n}\cup\mathcal{O}_{n}=[n]
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
n,M\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
n/M\geq 4
. Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}P\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{arb}}\big(\theta_{0},\Sigma,\epsilon,\pi\big)
, with corresponding observation patterns
\Omega_{1},\ldots,\Omega_{n}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. Randomly select
M
disjoint sets
(B_{m})_{m\in[M]}\subseteq[n]
such that
\lvert B_{m}\rvert=\lfloor n/M\rfloor
, and for
\theta=(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
m\in[M]
and
j\in[d]
, define

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}>0\}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}\coloneqq\frac{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}Z_{ij}}{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}}\cdot\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}+\theta_{j}\cdot(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}).

(81)

Then, writing
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}\coloneqq(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m1},\ldots,\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{md})^{\top}
, for all
m\in[M]
such that
B_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\tr\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\frac{2}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\tr\bigl(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr)+\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}

(82a)

and

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr]\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{6}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\big\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}.

(82b)

Proof.

We compute the entries of the matrix
(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}
, beginning with those on the diagonal. For
j\in[d]
, let

\displaystyle A_{jj}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{|B_{m}|q_{j}}{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}>0\}}\biggr)\leq 2,

(83)

where the inequality follows by the first part of Lemma
19
. Further, let
E_{jj}\coloneqq(1-q_{j})^{|B_{m}|}
. For
i\in\mathcal{I}_{n}
, we can write
Z_{i}=X_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{i}
, where
\mathbb{E}(X_{i})=\theta_{0}
,
\mathrm{Cov}(X_{i})=\Sigma
and
X_{i}\perp\!\!\!\perp\Omega_{i}
. Hence, for any
m\in[M]
such that
B_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{n}
and any
j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}\!-\!\theta_{0,j})^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})+(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj})(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}+\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj})^{2}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}(X_{ij}\!-\!\theta_{0,j})}{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}>0\}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}+\mathbb{P}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}=0)(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\Sigma_{jj}}{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}>0\}}\biggr)+(1-q_{j})^{|B_{m}|}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})^{2}

\displaystyle=A_{jj}\cdot\frac{\Sigma_{jj}^{\mathrm{IPW}}}{|B_{m}|}+E_{jj}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})^{2}.

(84)

Turning to the off-diagonal entries, for any
m\in[M]
such that
B_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{n}
and any distinct
j,k\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mk}-\theta_{0,k})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})+(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj})(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})\bigr\}\bigl\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mk}-\theta_{0,k})+(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k})\bigr\}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})\bigr)\bigl(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mk}-\theta_{0,k})\bigr)\big\}+\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj})(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk})(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k})\big\},

where in the final step, the cross-terms vanish as
\mathbb{E}(X_{i})=\theta_{0}
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
1\in B_{m}
. For the first term, we first define

\displaystyle A_{jk}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{(|B_{m}|q_{j})(|B_{m}|q_{k})}{\big(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ij}\big)\cdot\big(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ik}\big)}\Biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 2.56064pt\leq\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{(|B_{m}|q_{j})^{2}}{\bigl(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ij}\bigr)^{2}}\Biggr\}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{(|B_{m}|q_{k})^{2}}{\bigl(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ik}\bigr)^{2}}\Biggr\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{2|B_{m}|^{2}}{(|B_{m}|-1)^{2}}\leq 4,

(85)

where the first inequality follows from Cauchy–Schwarz and the second inequality follows from the second part of Lemma
19
, and the final inequality uses the fact that
|B_{m}|\geq 4
. We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}

\displaystyle(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})\cdot\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mk}-\theta_{0,k})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{jk}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{\bigl(\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ik}\bigr)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk}}{\big(\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}\big)\cdot\big(\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ik}\big)}\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{jk}\cdot|B_{m}|\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{\Omega_{1j}\Omega_{1k}}{\big(\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ij}\big)\cdot\big(\sum_{i\in B_{m}}\Omega_{ik}\big)}\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{jk}\cdot|B_{m}|\cdot\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1j}=\Omega_{1k}=1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{1}{\big(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ij}\big)\cdot\big(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ik}\big)}\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=A_{jk}\cdot\frac{\Sigma_{jk}q_{jk}}{|B_{m}|q_{j}q_{k}},

where the first equality follows from substituting the definition of
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}
on the event
\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}=1\}
(and similarly for
k
) and the second equality follows by symmetry.
For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj})(1-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk})(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mj}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\Omega\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{mk}=0)\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k})

\displaystyle=(1-q_{j}-q_{k}+q_{jk})^{|B_{m}|}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k})

\displaystyle\eqqcolon E_{jk}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k}).

Combining these two equalities then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mj}-\theta_{0,j})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{mk}-\theta_{0,k})\big]=A_{jk}\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}_{jk}+E_{jk}\cdot(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})(\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k}).

(86)

Therefore, by (
84
) and (
86
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}=\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot A\odot\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}+E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\},

where
A\coloneqq(A_{jk})_{j,k\in[d]}
and
E\coloneqq(E_{jk})_{j,k\in[d]}
. The desired inequality (
82a
) then follows as

\displaystyle\tr\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{d}A_{jj}\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}_{jj}+\sum_{j=1}^{d}E_{jj}(\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})+\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}},

where the inequality follows by (
83
) and Lemma
20
.

For inequality (
82b
), we define a matrix
A^{\prime}=(A_{jk}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
by
A^{\prime}_{jk}\coloneqq A_{jk}
for
j\neq k
and

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{jj}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\Biggl\{\frac{(|B_{m}|q_{j})^{2}}{\bigl(1+\sum_{i\in B_{m}\setminus\{1\}}\Omega_{ij}\bigr)^{2}}\Biggr\}\leq 2,

(87)

where the inequality follows from the second part of Lemma
19
and the assumption that
|B_{m}|\geq 4
.
Note that
A^{\prime}
is a positive semi-definite matrix, as it is the expectation of a positive semi-definite matrix. Now

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl\{(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\biggl\|\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot A\odot\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}+E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\biggr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\bigl\|A^{\prime}\odot\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\bigl\|(A-A^{\prime})\odot\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\bigl\|E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\|A^{\prime}\bigr\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{1}{|B_{m}|}\cdot\bigl\|A-A^{\prime}\bigr\|_{\infty}\bigl\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\bigl\|E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{6}{|B_{m}|}\bigl\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}},

where the first term in step
(i)
follows from Lemma
18
since
A^{\prime}
is positive semidefinite, the second term in step
(i)
follows since
A-A^{\prime}
is diagonal, and step
(ii)
follows from the inequalities (
83
), (
85
) and (
87
), as well as Lemma
20
.
∎

The following lemma can be deduced from
Horn 1990
, but for the convenience of the reader, we provide a short proof here.

Lemma 18
.

Let
A,B\in\mathcal{S}^{d\times d}
and further suppose that
A
is positive semi-definite. Then
\|A\odot B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.

Proof.

The proof largely follows that of
Horn 1990
. Since

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}A&A\\
A&A\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{S}^{2d\times 2d}\quad\text{and}\quad\begin{pmatrix}\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}I_{d}&B\\
B&\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}I_{d}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathcal{S}^{2d\times 2d}

are both positive semi-definite, by the Schur product theorem
(
Horn and Johnson 2012
, Theorem 7.5.3(a))
, their Hadamard product

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}(I_{d}\odot A)&A\odot B\\
A\odot B&\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}(I_{d}\odot A)\end{pmatrix}

is also positive semi-definite. Hence, for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\begin{pmatrix}v^{\top}&-v^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}(I_{d}\odot A)&A\odot B\\
A\odot B&\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}(I_{d}\odot A)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v\\
-v\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=2\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}v^{\top}(I\odot A)v-2v^{\top}(A\odot B)v,

so
\|A\odot B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|I\odot A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|A\|_{\infty}\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

Lemma 19
.

Let
Y\sim\mathsf{Bin}(n,q)
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\in(0,1]
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(Y^{-1}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y>0\}}\bigr)\leq\frac{2}{nq}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(Y+1)^{-2}\bigr\}\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}q^{2}}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(Y+1)^{-1}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=0}^{n}(y+1)^{-1}\binom{n}{y}q^{y}(1-q)^{n-y}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=0}^{n}\frac{1}{q(n+1)}\binom{n+1}{y+1}q^{y+1}(1-q)^{n-y}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q(n+1)}\sum_{y=1}^{n+1}\binom{n+1}{y}q^{y}(1-q)^{n+1-y}\leq\frac{1}{nq}.

The first inequality in the statement then follows as
y^{-1}\leq 2(y+1)^{-1}
for all
y\geq 1
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(Y+1)^{-1}(Y+2)^{-1}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=0}^{n}(y+1)^{-1}(y+2)^{-1}\binom{n}{y}q^{y}(1-q)^{n-y}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y=0}^{n}\frac{1}{q^{2}(n+1)(n+2)}\binom{n+2}{y+2}q^{y+2}(1-q)^{n-y}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}q^{2}}.

The second inequality in the statement then follows since
(y+1)^{-2}\leq 2(y+1)^{-1}(y+2)^{-1}
for all
y\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 20
.

Under the set up in the proof of Lemma
17
, we have

\displaystyle\|E\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}.

Proof.

We will make use of the following inequality

\displaystyle(1-x)^{k}\leq\frac{1}{ekx}\quad\text{for all }x\in(0,1]\text{ and }k\in\mathbb{N}.

(88)

To see this, note that
k\log(1-x)\leq-kx\leq-\log(kx)-1
. Hence, for each
j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle E_{jj}=(1-q_{j})^{|B_{m}|}\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}},

and for each
j,k\in[d]
,

\displaystyle E_{jk}=(1-q_{j}-q_{k}+q_{jk})^{|B_{m}|}\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|(q_{j}+q_{k}-q_{jk})}\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}},

where the final inequality follows since
q_{jk}\leq q_{k}
, so that
q_{j}+q_{k}-q_{jk}\geq q_{j}\geq q_{\min}
. This establishes the first inequality.

For the second bound, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl[E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr]_{jk}\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}\cdot|\theta_{j}-\theta_{0,j}|\cdot|\theta_{k}-\theta_{0,k}|.

Hence

\displaystyle\bigl\|E\odot\bigl\{(\theta-\theta_{0})(\theta-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{1}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}}\bigl\||\theta-\theta_{0}|\cdot|\theta-\theta_{0}|^{\top}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{e|B_{m}|q_{\min}},

where
|\theta-\theta_{0}|
denotes the entrywise absolute value, and the inequality follows from the fact
6
6

6

To see this, observe that
v^{\top}Av\leq|v|^{\top}|A||v|\leq|v|^{\top}B|v|
for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
|A|
denotes the entrywise absolute value of
A
.
that if
A=(A_{jk}),B=(B_{jk})\in\mathcal{S}^{d\times d}
are such that
|A_{jk}|\leq B_{jk}
for all
j,k\in[d]
, then
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

We require the following lemma, which is an analogue of
Depersin and Lecué 2022b
. We state and prove it here as we require slight changes in several parts of the proof.

Lemma 21
.

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}P\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{arb}}\big(\theta_{0},\Sigma,\epsilon,\pi\big)
. Let
n\geq 5
,
\delta\in[2e^{-(n-4)/720{,}000},1)
and
\epsilon\in[0,1/3000]
. Take
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\frac{n}{4}\geq M\geq 300\bigl(2\epsilon n+\log(2/\delta)\bigr)\vee 180{,}000\log(2/\delta),

and for each
m\in[M]
, let
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}
be as in (
81
), for some
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there are at least
99M/100
blocks
m
such that

\lvert v^{\top}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})\rvert\leq 4000\sqrt{\frac{\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})}{n}}+100\sqrt{\frac{M\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{n}}+30\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{M}{nq_{\min}}}\eqqcolon r^{\mathrm{IPW}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
38
, we find that
\mathcal{E}_{1}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{O}_{n}|\leq 2\epsilon n+\log(2/\delta)\}
occurs with probability at least
1-\delta/2
. Henceforth we will work on this event. By definition of
M
, there are at most
M/300
blocks that are contaminated. Let
(\overline{Y}_{m})_{m\in[M]}
denote the uncontaminated block means so that
(\overline{Y}_{m})_{m\in[M]}
are independent and identically distributed, and at most
M/300
of the vectors
(\overline{Y}_{m})_{m\in[M]}
and
(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m})_{m\in[M]}
are not equal. Let

\displaystyle\Gamma\coloneqq|B_{m}|\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}\bigr\}\quad\text{and}\quad r^{\prime}\coloneqq 2800\sqrt{\frac{\tr(\Gamma)}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{1200\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{|B_{m}|}}.

We begin by establishing that the claim of the lemma holds with
r^{\mathrm{IPW}}
replaced by
r^{\prime}
. To this end, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}\}}\leq\frac{M}{150}.

Define
\psi:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
by

\displaystyle\psi(t)\coloneqq\begin{cases}0\quad&\text{if }t<1/2\\
2(t-1/2)&\text{if }1/2\leq t<1\\
1&\text{if }t\geq 1,\end{cases}

so that
\psi
is
2
-Lipschitz and
\mathbbm{1}_{\{t>1\}}\leq\psi(t)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{t>1/2\}}
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}\}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\Bigl\{\mathbbm{1}_{\{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}\}}-\mathbb{P}\bigl(|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}/2\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;+\mathbb{P}\bigl(|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}/2\bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\biggl\{\psi\biggl(\frac{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|}{r^{\prime}}\biggr)-\mathbb{E}\psi\biggl(\frac{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|}{r^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;+\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{P}\bigl(|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}/2\bigr)\eqqcolon A+B.

Towards bounding
B
, we apply Markov’s inequality to obtain that for every
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|>r^{\prime}/2\bigr)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\bigl\{v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})^{\top}v\bigr\}}{(r^{\prime}/2)^{2}}\leq\frac{\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{|B_{m}|(r^{\prime}/2)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{300},

where the final inequality follows from the definition of
r^{\prime}
. Therefore,
B\leq M/300
. For the first term, we have
A=(A-\mathbb{E}A)+\mathbb{E}A
. By the bounded differences inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 2.9.1)
and our choice of
M
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta/2
that

A-\mathbb{E}A\leq\sqrt{\frac{M\log(2/\delta)}{2}}\leq\frac{M}{600}.

Now let
\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{M}
be independent Rademacher random variables. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\biggl\{\psi\biggl(\frac{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|}{r^{\prime}}\biggr)-\mathbb{E}\psi\biggl(\frac{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|}{r^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\leq}2\mathbb{E}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\varepsilon_{m}\psi\biggl(\frac{|v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})|}{r^{\prime}}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\leq}4\mathbb{E}\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\varepsilon_{m}\cdot\frac{v^{\top}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})}{r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{r^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl\|\sum_{m=1}^{M}\varepsilon_{m}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})\biggr\|_{2}\biggr\}\leq\frac{4}{r^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\bigg\|\sum_{m=1}^{M}\varepsilon_{m}(\overline{Y}_{m}-\theta_{0})\bigg\|_{2}^{2}\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{r^{\prime}}\sqrt{\frac{\tr(\Gamma)M}{|B_{m}|}}\leq\frac{M}{600},

where step
(i)
follows from (one-sided) symmetrisation
(see, e.g.,
Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart 2013
, Lemma 11.4)
, step
(ii)
follows from Talagrand’s contraction inequality
(e.g.,
Vershynin 2018
, Exercise 6.7.7)
since
\psi(|\cdot|)
is 2-Lipschitz, and the final inequality follows from the definition of
r^{\prime}
. The claim, with
r^{\mathrm{IPW}}
replaced by
r^{\prime}
, follows by a union bound. The final result then follows from applying Lemma
17
to bound both
\tr(\Gamma)
and
\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, using the facts that
|B_{m}|\geq 3n/(4M)
and
M\geq 180{,}000\log 2
, together with the inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
for
x,y\geq 0
.
∎

The following corollary is a variant of
Depersin and Lecué 2022b
.

Corollary 22
.

In the setting of Lemma
21
, and with
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{1},\ldots,\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{M}
defined as in Lemma
17
for some
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, take

\widetilde{\theta}_{n}\coloneqq\textsc{Robust\_Block\_Descent}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{1},\ldots,\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{M})

from Algorithm
4
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widetilde{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}\leq 4\cdot 10^{6}\sqrt{\frac{\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})}{n}}+9\cdot 10^{4}\sqrt{\frac{M\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{n}}+3\cdot 10^{4}\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{M}{nq_{\min}}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}
denote the event on which for all
A\in\mathcal{S}^{d\times d}_{+}
with
\tr(A)=1
, there are at least
9M/10
blocks for which
\|A^{1/2}(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.05pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.12498pt]{5.21706pt}{0.43057pt}}{Z}_{m}-\theta_{0})\|_{2}\leq 8r^{\mathrm{IPW}}
. We claim that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-e^{-M/180{,}000}
.

The proof of the claim follows that of
Depersin and Lecué 2022b
, replacing their Lemma 1 with our Lemma
21
and their
r
with our
r^{\mathrm{IPW}}
. Moreover, the remainder of the proof of
Depersin and Lecué 2022b
then carries over on our event
\mathcal{E}
.
∎

Equipped with these preliminary lemmas, we turn to the proof of Theorem
3
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Let
S^{(1)},\ldots,S^{(T)}
be as in Algorithm
1
. For
t\in[T]
, let
\epsilon^{(t)}
denote the proportion of outliers, or contaminated observations, in the set
S^{(t)}
. Then, combining Lemma
38
and a union bound, we deduce that with probability at least
1-\delta/3
,

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[T]}\,\epsilon^{(t)}\leq 2\epsilon+\frac{\log(3T/\delta)}{\lfloor n/T\rfloor}\leq 2\epsilon+\frac{2T\log(3T/\delta)}{n}\eqqcolon\epsilon^{\prime}.

Henceforth we work on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\coloneqq\bigl\{\max_{t\in[T]}\,\epsilon^{(t)}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}\bigr\}
.

Let
M
be as in Algorithm
1
. Then, if we take
A_{2}=1200
and
A_{3}=180,000
in Algorithm
1
, it follows that for any
t\in[T]
,

\frac{n}{4T}\geq M\geq 300\biggl(\frac{2\epsilon^{(t)}n}{T}+\log(6T/\delta)\biggr)\vee 180,000\log(6T/\delta).

Moreover, we have both
\delta/(3T)\geq 2e^{-n/(720{,}000T)}
and
\max_{t\in[T]}\,\epsilon^{(t)}\leq 1/3000
. We therefore apply Corollary
22
by first conditioning on
\widehat{\theta}^{(t)}
, with
\theta
and
n
in that statement taken to be
\widehat{\theta}^{(t)}
and
\lfloor n/T\rfloor
respectively, and then taking a further expectation, to find that for each
t\in[T-1]
, there is an event
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}
with
\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)})^{c}\bigr)\leq\delta/(3T)
such that on
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(t+1)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 5\cdot 10^{6}\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{T\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{TM\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{n}}\biggr)+4\cdot 10^{4}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(t)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{TM}{nq_{\min}}}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon a+b\cdot\|\widehat{\theta}^{(t)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}.

By the assumed lower bound on
q_{\min}
, we have
b\leq 1/2
. Moreover, since Algorithm
1
is initialised with the coordinate-wise trimmed mean and by assumption
q_{\min}\geq 8\log(6d/\delta)/n
, we combine
Lugosi and Mendelson 2021
with Lemma
38
(b)
and a union bound to obtain that there is an event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
with
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c})\leq\delta/3
such that on
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(1)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{14000T\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})\log(24d/\delta)}{n}+9216\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}.

Thus, taking
A_{1}=10^{-9}
, we find that
2^{-(T-1)}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(1)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq a
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(T)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{T-2}ab^{\ell}+b^{T-1}\cdot\|\widehat{\theta}^{(1)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq 2a+2^{-(T-1)}\cdot\|\widehat{\theta}^{(1)}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq 3a.

Hence,
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\bigcap_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\bigr)\geq 1-\delta
and on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\bigcap_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq 3a=1.5\cdot 10^{7}\cdot\biggl(\sqrt{\frac{T\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{TM\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{n}}\biggr),

with probability at least
1-\delta
. The final bound then follows upon substituting the definition of
M
into the display above.
∎

C.2
Proof of Theorem
4

Proof of Theorem
4
.

First, note that when
\epsilon=0
, by Proposition
47
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\gtrsim\frac{\tr(\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}})}{n}+\frac{\|\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(1/\delta)}{n}.

(89)

Now we consider the case
\epsilon\in\bigl(0,\frac{q_{\min}}{1+q_{\min}}\bigr)
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}_{11}=\max_{j\in[d]}\Sigma^{\mathrm{IPW}}_{jj}
, and let
a\coloneqq(\alpha+\alpha^{2})/2
and
b\coloneqq(3\alpha+\alpha^{2})/2

for some
\alpha\in(0,1/3]
to be chosen later. Define random vectors
X^{(1)}=(X^{(1)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(1)}_{d})^{\top}\sim P^{(1)}
and
X^{(2)}=(X^{(2)}_{1},\ldots,X^{(2)}_{d})^{\top}\sim P^{(2)}
with independent components satisfying

\displaystyle X^{(1)}_{1}\coloneqq\begin{cases}-\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{11}}{2\alpha}}\quad&\text{with probability }\alpha\\
0&\text{with probability }1-2\alpha,\\
\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{11}}{2\alpha}}\quad&\text{with probability }\alpha\end{cases}\ \,X^{(2)}_{1}\coloneqq\begin{cases}-\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{11}}{2\alpha}}\quad&\text{with probability }a\\
0&\text{with probability }1-a-b\\
\sqrt{\frac{\Sigma_{11}}{2\alpha}}\quad&\text{with probability }b,\end{cases}

and
X^{(1)}_{j}\overset{d}{=}X^{(2)}_{j}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\Sigma_{jj})
for
j\in\{2,\ldots,d\}
. Then

\Var(X^{(2)}_{1})=\frac{(a+b+2ab-a^{2}-b^{2})\Sigma_{11}}{2\alpha}=\Sigma_{11}.

Thus
\Cov(X^{(1)})=\Cov(X^{(2)})=\Sigma
, so
P^{(\ell)}\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(X^{(\ell)}),\Sigma\bigr)
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, and

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(X^{(1)})-\mathbb{E}(X^{(2)})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{\alpha\Sigma_{11}}{2}.

Moreover, by Lemma
45
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(1)})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(2)})}\bigl)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ATV}(P^{(1)},P^{(2)},\pi)=\sum_{S:1\in S}\pi(S)\cdot\mathrm{TV}\bigl(P^{(1)}_{S},P^{(2)}_{S}\bigr)

\displaystyle=q_{1}\cdot\mathrm{TV}\bigl(P^{(1)}_{1},P^{(2)}_{1}\bigr)=\frac{q_{1}}{2}\biggl(\frac{\alpha-\alpha^{2}}{2}+\alpha^{2}+\frac{\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{2}\biggr)\leq q_{1}\alpha.

We then pick
\alpha=\epsilon/(3q_{1})<1/3
since
\epsilon<q_{\min}
so that

\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(1)})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(2)})}\bigl)\leq\epsilon\leq\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon},\quad\text{and}\quad\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(X^{(1)})-\mathbb{E}(X^{(2)})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{\epsilon\Sigma_{11}^{\mathrm{IPW}}}{6}.

Consequently, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\geq\frac{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(X^{(1)})-\mathbb{E}(X^{(2)})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}}{4}=\frac{\epsilon\Sigma_{11}^{\mathrm{IPW}}}{24}.

(90)

Combining (
89
) and (
90
) yields the desired result.

Next, we consider the case where
\epsilon\geq\frac{q_{\min}}{1+q_{\min}}
. Without loss of generality, assume that
q_{1}=q_{\min}
. Let
\theta^{(1)}\coloneqq(2t^{1/2},0,\ldots,0)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for some
t>0
and let
\theta^{(2)}\coloneqq 0\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Writing
P^{(1)}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(\theta^{(1)},\Sigma)
and
P^{(2)}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(\theta^{(2)},\Sigma)
, we have by Lemma
45
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(1)})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\pi,P^{(2)})}\bigl)=\mathrm{ATV}(P^{(1)},P^{(2)};\pi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S:1\in S}\pi(S)\cdot\mathrm{TV}(P^{(1)}_{S},P_{S}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=q_{1}\mathrm{TV}\bigl(P^{(1)}_{\{1\}},P^{(2)}_{\{1\}}\bigr)\leq q_{1}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}.

Hence, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, we see that
\mathcal{M}\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\geq t
. Since
t>0
was arbitrary, the result follows.
∎

C.3
Univariate arbitrary contamination lower bounds

The lower bounds in Proposition
23
are presented primarily to ensure the completeness of Table
1
. Corresponding upper bounds are attained by the median in the Gaussian case
(
Chen, Gao and Ren 2018
, Theorem 2.1)
, and a trimmed mean
(
Lugosi and Mendelson 2021
, Theorem 1 and the subsequent remark)
in the sub-Gaussian case, in both cases applied to the observed data.

Proposition 23
.

Let
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
q\in(0,1]
,
\sigma>0
,
\delta\in(0,1/4]
and
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
.

(a)

Let
\Theta\coloneqq\mathbb{R}
and let
\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\coloneqq\bigl\{P_{0}^{\otimes n}:P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{arb}}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(\theta,\sigma^{2}),\epsilon,q\bigr)\bigr\}
for
\theta\in\Theta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\begin{cases}\gtrsim\dfrac{\sigma^{2}\log(1/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}\quad&\text{if }\epsilon<\frac{q}{1+q}\\
=\infty&\text{if }\epsilon\geq\frac{q}{1+q}.\end{cases}

(b)

Let
\Theta\coloneqq\mathbb{R}
and let
\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\coloneqq\bigl\{P_{0}^{\otimes n}:P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{arb}}(P,\epsilon,q),\,P\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{2}}(\theta,\sigma^{2})\bigr\}
for
\theta\in\Theta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\begin{cases}\gtrsim\dfrac{\sigma^{2}\log(1/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}\log(1/\kappa)\quad&\text{if }\epsilon<\frac{q}{1+q}\\
=\infty&\text{if }\epsilon\geq\frac{q}{1+q}.\end{cases}

Proof.

(a)
First consider the case where
\epsilon<\frac{q}{1+q}
. Let
X_{1}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\sigma^{2})\eqqcolon P_{1}
and
X_{2}\sim\mathsf{N}(2\sigma\kappa,\sigma^{2})\eqqcolon P_{2}
. By Pinsker’s inequality,
\mathrm{TV}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},P_{2})}=\kappa
, so that by Lemma
45
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{1})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{2})}\bigr)=q\cdot\mathrm{TV}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}.

Hence, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{(\mathbb{E}X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{2})^{2}}{4}=\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}.

(91)

Further, by choosing the contamination distribution
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
such that
Q\bigl(\{\star\}\bigr)=1
and applying Proposition
46
(a)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\gtrsim\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(1/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}.

(92)

Combining (
91
) and (
92
) yields the lower bound for
\epsilon<\frac{q}{1+q}
.

Next consider the case where
\epsilon\geq\frac{q}{1+q}
. Let
a>0
,
X_{1}\sim P_{1}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and
X_{2}\sim P_{2}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(a\sigma,\sigma^{2})
. By Lemma
45
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{1})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{2})}\bigr)=q\cdot\mathrm{TV}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq q\leq\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}.

Hence, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{(\mathbb{E}X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{2})^{2}}{4}=\frac{\sigma^{2}a^{2}}{4}.

Since this holds for all
a>0
, we deduce that
\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})=\infty
in this case.

(b)
Let
c_{1}>0
be a universal constant that will be specified later. Define
P_{1}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
by
P_{1}\bigl((t,\infty)\bigr)\coloneqq e^{-t^{2}/(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}
for
t\geq 0
. Define
P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
by

\displaystyle P_{2}(\{0\})\coloneqq\kappa,\quad P_{2}\bigl((t,\infty)\bigr)\coloneqq\begin{cases}e^{-t^{2}/(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}\;&\text{if }0\leq t\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}\\
0\;&\text{if }t>c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}.\end{cases}

Let
X_{1}\sim P_{1}
and
X_{2}\sim P_{2}
. Since
\mathbb{P}(|X_{\ell}|\geq t)\leq e^{-t^{2}/(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}
for
t\geq 0
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, we have by
Vershynin 2018
that
\|X_{\ell}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq c_{1}C_{2}\sigma
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, where
C_{2}>0
is a universal constant. Thus by
Vershynin 2018
, there exists a universal constant
C_{3}>0
such that
\|X_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}X_{\ell}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq c_{1}C_{2}C_{3}\sigma
. Hence, taking
c_{1}\coloneqq(C_{2}C_{3})^{-1}
, we have
P_{\ell}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{2}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(X_{\ell}),\sigma^{2}\bigr)
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
.
Moreover,
\mathrm{TV}(P_{1},P_{2})=P_{2}(\{0\})=\kappa
, so that by Lemma
45
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{1})},\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q,P_{2})}\bigr)=q\cdot\mathrm{TV}(P_{1},P_{2})=\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}.

Now, integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}}^{\infty}x\cdot\frac{2x}{(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}e^{-x^{2}/(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\kappa\cdot c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}+\int_{c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}}^{\infty}e^{-x^{2}/(c_{1}\sigma)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\gtrsim\sigma\kappa\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}.

Hence, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{(\mathbb{E}X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{2})^{2}}{4}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}\log(1/\kappa).

(93)

By (
93
) and applying Proposition
46
(a)
with contamination distribution
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
satisfying
Q(\{\star\})=1
as in
(a)
, we obtain the desired lower bound for
\epsilon<\frac{q}{1+q}
. Finally, the lower bound for
\epsilon\geq\frac{q}{1+q}
follows from part
(a)
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs from Section
4

D.1
Proofs from Section
4.1

D.1.1
Proof of Theorem
5

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Given
R\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})
, there exists a random vector
(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{(1)},\Omega_{0}^{(2)},W_{0})
taking values in
\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}^{3}
such that
W_{0}\perp\!\!\!\perp(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{(1)},\Omega_{0}^{(2)})
,
W_{0}\sim\mathsf{Ber}(\epsilon)
,
\Omega_{0}^{(1)}\perp\!\!\!\perp(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{(2)})
,
\Omega_{0}^{(1)}\sim\mathsf{Ber}(q)
,
X_{0}\sim\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
and

R=\mathsf{Law}\bigl((1-W_{0})\cdot X_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{0}^{(1)}+W_{0}\cdot X_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{0}^{(2)}\bigr).

Note that if
Z_{0}\coloneqq(1-W_{0})\cdot X_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{0}^{(1)}+W_{0}\cdot X_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{0}^{(2)}
, then
Z_{0}\mid\{W_{0}=0\}\sim\mathsf{MCAR}_{(\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2}),q)}
. We then generate
(X_{i},\Omega_{i}^{(1)},\Omega_{i}^{(2)},W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathsf{Law}(X_{0},\Omega_{0}^{(1)},\Omega_{0}^{(2)},W_{0})
, and set
Z_{i}\coloneqq(1-W_{i})\cdot X_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{i}^{(1)}+W_{i}\cdot X_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{i}^{(2)}
for
i\in[n]
, so that
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}R
.

Now define the inliers as
\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\{i\in[n]:W_{i}=0\}
, the outliers as
\mathcal{O}\coloneqq\{i\in[n]:W_{i}=1\}
, and the observed indices as
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\{i\in[n]:Z_{i}\neq\star\}
. Equipped with this notation, we note the following pair of structural properties

\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}}\,X_{i}\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\,Z_{i}\leq\max_{i\in(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}}\,X_{i}\quad\text{ and }\quad\min_{i\in(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}}\,X_{i}\leq\min_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\,Z_{i}\leq\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}}\,X_{i}.

We deduce the sandwich relation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Bigl(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}}\,X_{i}+\min_{i\in(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}}\,X_{i}\Bigr)\leq\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{AE}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Bigl(\max_{i\in(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}}\,X_{i}+\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}}\,X_{i}\Bigr).

(94)

Now
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and
\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}
are independent, and similarly
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and
\mathcal{O}
are independent, so
(X_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}}|(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
and
(X_{i})_{i\in(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}}|\bigl((\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}\bigr)\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
. We let
N_{1}\coloneqq\lvert\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D}\rvert
and
N_{2}\coloneqq\lvert(\mathcal{I}\cap\mathcal{D})\cup\mathcal{O}\rvert
, and define

B_{\ell}\coloneqq\sigma\sqrt{2\log{N_{\ell}}}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\cdot\frac{\log{\log{N_{\ell}}}+\log(4\pi)}{\sqrt{2\log{N_{\ell}}}},

for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}\coloneqq\{N_{1}\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2\}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{O}|\leq 3n\epsilon\}
. By
Tanguy 2015
, there exists a universal constant
C_{1}^{\prime}>0
such that for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\Bigl|\max_{i\in[N_{\ell}]}X_{i}-\theta-B_{\ell}\Bigr|\geq\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}N_{\ell}}\biggr\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}_{1}\;\bigg|\;N_{\ell}\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{4}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\Bigl|\min_{i\in[N_{\ell}]}X_{i}-\theta+B_{\ell}\Bigr|\geq\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}N_{\ell}}\biggr\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}_{1}\;\bigg|\;N_{\ell}\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta}{4}.

Combining these inequalities with the sandwich relation (
94
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl\{\bigl\lvert\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{AE}}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr\rvert\geq B_{2}-B_{1}+\frac{2C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}N_{1}}\biggr\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}_{1}\;\bigg|\;N_{1},N_{2}\biggr)\leq\frac{2\delta}{3}.

(95)

Using the inequality
\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\leq(a-b)/\sqrt{b}
for
0<b<a
and the fact that
x\mapsto\frac{\log\log x+\log(4\pi)}{\log^{1/2}x}
is decreasing for
x\geq\exp\bigl(\frac{e^{2}}{4\pi}\bigr)
, we deduce that on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\displaystyle B_{2}-B_{1}\leq\frac{2\sigma\log(N_{2}/N_{1})}{\log^{1/2}N_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma\log\bigl(1+3n\epsilon/N_{1}\bigr)}{\log^{1/2}N_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\log^{1/2}{\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)/2\bigr)}}\leq\frac{3\sigma\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}.

(96)

Now, we first assume that
\epsilon\geq n^{-1}\log(4/\delta)
. By Lemma
38
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\delta/2
, since by assumption,
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq 8\log(4/\delta)
. Moreover, combining the inequalities (
95
) and (
D.1.1
) yields that on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{AE}}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr\rvert\leq\frac{3\sigma\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}+\frac{2C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}N_{1}}\leq C_{1}\sigma\cdot\frac{\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)+\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)},

(97)

where
C_{1}\coloneqq 3(1+C_{1}^{\prime})
. Hence, (
97
) holds with probability at least
1-\delta
when
\epsilon\geq n^{-1}\log(4/\delta)
. Finally, consider the case in which
\epsilon<n^{-1}\log(4/\delta)
. Then, since
\mathcal{R}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2}),\epsilon,q\bigr)\subseteq\mathcal{R}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2}),n^{-1}\log(4/\delta),q\bigr)
, we have by (
97
) that

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{AE}}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sigma\cdot\frac{\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)+\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}\leq C_{1}\sigma\cdot\frac{\frac{6\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sigma\cdot\frac{2\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}\leq 2C_{1}\sigma\cdot\frac{\log\bigl(1+\frac{6\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)+\log(8/\delta)}{\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)},

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
∎

D.1.2
Proof of Theorem
6

(a)

The two black curves are
\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}
and
\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(a,\sigma)}
respectively, as labelled in the figure. The blue curve is
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}
, and the orange curve is
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(a,\sigma)}
.

(b)

The curve above the blue region illustrates the function
f_{1}
in (
99
).

(c)

The curve above the orange region illustrates the function
f_{2}
in (
100
).

Figure 6:
Construction of the lower bound in Theorem
6
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Consider the construction illustrated in Figure
6
. For
a>0
to be specified later, let

\displaystyle\tau\coloneqq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2a}\cdot\log\bigg(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigg)

(98)

denote the unique point in
\mathbb{R}
where
\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(\tau)=q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(\tau)
. Next, define the function
f_{1}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle f_{1}(x)\coloneqq\begin{cases}q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }x\leq 0\\
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }0<x\leq\tau\\
\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }x>\tau.\end{cases}

(99)

Similarly, we note that
-\tau
is the unique point satisfying
\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(-\tau)=q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(-\tau)
and define the function
f_{2}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle f_{2}(x)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }x\leq-\tau\\
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }-\tau<x\leq 0\\
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(x)&\text{ if }x>0.\end{cases}

(100)

Note that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{\ell}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\leq 1
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, so we may construct
P_{1},P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
with Radon–Nikodym derivatives

\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\ell}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\star}}(z)\coloneqq f_{\ell}(z)\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}\}}+\biggl(1-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{\ell}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\biggr)\mathbbm{1}_{\{z=\star\}}\quad\text{for}\quad\ell\in\{1,2\},

where
\lambda_{\star}
denotes the extension of the Lebesgue measure to
\mathbb{R}_{\star}
as defined in Section
1.2
. Then, by Proposition
2
,
P_{1}\in\mathcal{R}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(-a,\sigma^{2}),\epsilon,q\bigr)
and
P_{2}\in\mathcal{R}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(a,\sigma^{2}),\epsilon,q\bigr)
. Since
P_{1}(\{\star\})=P_{2}(\{\star\})
and
f_{1}(x)=f_{2}(x)
for
x\in[-\tau,\tau]
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2})=\int_{-\infty}^{-\tau}q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)\log\biggl(\frac{q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)}{\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(x)}\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)\log\biggl(\frac{\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(x)}{q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(x)}\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\biggl\{\frac{2a^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}-\log\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\}\bigl\{1-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\biggl\{\frac{2a^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\log\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\}\bigl\{1-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2a\bigl[q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{2aq(1-\epsilon)}{\sigma^{2}}(a-\tau)\bigl\{1-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)\bigr\}+\frac{2a\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}}{\sigma^{2}}(a+\tau)\bigl\{1-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2a\bigl[q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)\bigr].

Next, set

\displaystyle a\coloneqq\frac{\sigma}{4}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\log^{-1/2}{\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}>0,

so that by substituting this definition into (
98
), we obtain

\tau=2\sigma\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad a\leq\frac{\tau}{8},

where the inequality follows from our assumption (
69
). Hence, by the Mills ratio bound
1-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(x)\leq\sigma^{2}\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(x)/x
for
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},P_{2})

\displaystyle\leq 2a\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2a\bigl[q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau+a)\bigr]

\displaystyle=2aq(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{2}\cdot\log\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\log^{-1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\cdot q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(\tau-a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma}{2}\cdot\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\cdot q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\phi_{(0,\sigma)}(7\tau/8)

\displaystyle=\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\cdot\exp\biggl\{-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Bigl(\frac{7}{8}\Bigr)^{2}\cdot 4\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\log^{1/2}\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)\cdot\bigl\{nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr\}^{-3/2}\leq\frac{1}{5n}.

Thus,
\mathrm{KL}(P_{1}^{\otimes n},P_{2}^{\otimes n})\leq 1/5<\log\bigl(\frac{1}{4\delta(1-\delta)}\bigr)
for
\delta\in(0,1/4]
, so by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, we deduce that for
\delta\in(0,1/4]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2}\bigr)\geq a^{2}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\log^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{16\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}.

(101)

Finally, note that
\mathsf{MCAR}_{(q(1-\epsilon),\mathsf{N}(\theta,\sigma^{2}))}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{N}(\theta,\sigma^{2}),\epsilon,q)
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
, since we can choose the contamination distribution
Q
such that
Q(\{\star\})=1
. Therefore, by Proposition
46
(a)
, we have that for
\delta\in(0,1/4]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2}\bigr)\begin{cases}\geq\dfrac{\sigma^{2}\log(1/\delta)}{20nq(1-\epsilon)}\quad&\text{if }\delta\geq\dfrac{\{1-q(1-\epsilon)\}^{n}}{2}\\
=\infty\quad&\text{if }\delta<\dfrac{\{1-q(1-\epsilon)\}^{n}}{2}.\end{cases}

(102)

Combining (
101
) and (
102
) yields the desired result.
∎

D.1.3
Proof of Theorem
7

In order to prove Theorem
7
, we require a preliminary lemma.

Lemma 24
.

Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
be distinct, and set
a\coloneqq\lvert\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\rvert/2
. Then, writing
b\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\log\bigl(1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)
, there exists a continuous and strictly increasing function
f_{\mathrm{K},b}:(0,\infty)\to(0,1]
such that

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})\bigr)\geq f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a).

Moreover,

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\frac{a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\quad\text{when }b\leq 1/2,

and

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\quad\text{when }b>1/2.

Proof.

Since
d_{\mathrm{K}}
is translation invariant, we may assume without loss of generality that
\theta_{1}=-a
and
\theta_{2}=a
. By Proposition
2
, if
R_{\ell}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{\ell})
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, then each admits a density
h_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}_{\star}\to\mathbb{R}
with respect to the extended Lebesgue measure
\lambda_{\star}
such that
h_{\ell}(x)/\phi_{(\theta_{\ell},\sigma)}(x)\in[q(1-\epsilon),\,q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon]
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
\tau\coloneqq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2a}\cdot\log\bigl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}b}{a}
,
so that
q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(-\tau)=\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(-\tau)
, see Figure
6
.

When
b\leq 1/2
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\inf_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\,R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})}\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}|R_{1}(A)-R_{2}(A)|

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\,R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})}\bigl\{R_{1}\bigl((-\infty,-\sigma^{2}b/a]\bigr)-R_{2}\bigl((-\infty,-\sigma^{2}b/a]\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi_{(\theta_{1},\sigma)}(-\sigma^{2}b/a)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi_{(\theta_{2},\sigma)}(-\sigma^{2}b/a)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\eqqcolon f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a).

Now
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is continuously differentiable, with

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{K},b}^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\biggl(\frac{1}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a^{2}}\biggr)\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\Bigl(-\frac{1}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a^{2}}\Bigr)\phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle>\biggl(\frac{1}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a^{2}}\biggr)\cdot\sigma\biggl\{q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}\biggl(-\frac{\sigma^{2}b}{a}\biggr)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\phi_{(a,\sigma)}\biggl(-\frac{\sigma^{2}b}{a}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\biggl(\frac{1}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a^{2}}\biggr)\cdot\sigma\Bigl(q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\phi_{(-a,\sigma)}(-\tau)-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\phi_{(a,\sigma)}(-\tau)\Bigr)=0,

so that
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is strictly increasing as well.
Moreover,

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\biggl\{\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\biggr\}-\epsilon\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\frac{2a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\epsilon\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr),

(103)

where the final inequality follows from the mean value theorem
\Phi\bigl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\bigr)-\Phi\bigl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\bigr)=\frac{2a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi(x^{\prime})\geq\frac{2a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\bigl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\bigr)
, where
x^{\prime}\in\bigl[-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a},\,\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\bigr]
. Next notice that

\displaystyle\epsilon\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{a/\sigma+\sigma b/a}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon a}{\sigma b}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\frac{a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr),

(104)

where the first inequality follows from the Mills ratio bound
\Phi(-x)\leq\phi(x)/x
for
x>0
, and the final inequality follows from the fact that
\log(1+x)\geq x/2
for
x\in[0,2]
, so that
b=\frac{1}{2}\log\bigl(1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
. Therefore, by (
103
) and (
104
) we deduce that

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\frac{a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr),

when
b\leq 1/2
.

On the other hand, when
b>1/2
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{1}),\,R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\theta_{2})}\bigl\{R_{1}\bigl((-\infty,-2\sigma^{2}b/a]\bigr)-R_{2}\bigl((-\infty,-2\sigma^{2}b/a]\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi_{(\theta_{1},\sigma)}(-2\sigma^{2}b/a)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi_{(\theta_{2},\sigma)}(-2\sigma^{2}b/a)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\eqqcolon f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a).

Similarly to the previous case,
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is continuously differentiable and strictly increasing.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7
.

We first derive an upper bound on
d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)
. Let
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\{i\in[n]:Z_{i}\neq\star\}
and
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}\neq\star)
, so that with the convention that
0/0\coloneqq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}|\widehat{R}_{n}(A)-R(A)|

\displaystyle=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\biggl|\frac{|\mathcal{D}|}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{i}\in A\}}-\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\cdot\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}\in A|Z_{1}\neq\star)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{D}|}{n}\cdot\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\biggl|\frac{1}{\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{i}\in A\}}-\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}\in A|Z_{1}\neq\star)\biggr|+\biggl|\frac{|\mathcal{D}|}{n}-\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\biggr|.

(105)

Now, since
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\geq q(1-\epsilon)
, we have by our lower bound on
\delta
that

\log\Bigl(\frac{4}{\delta}\Bigr)\leq\frac{\bigl\{nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr\}^{31/36}}{6400\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}\leq\frac{nq(1-\epsilon)}{6400}\leq\frac{n\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}}{6400}.

Hence, by Bernstein’s inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 2.8.4)
, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{|\mathcal{D}|}{n}-\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\biggr|\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}<\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}.

(106)

Furthermore, by the Dvoretzky–Kiefer–Wolfowitz–Massart inequality
(
Massart 1990
;
Reeve 2024
)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\biggl|\frac{1}{\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{i}\in A\}}-\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}\in A\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\biggr|\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{2|\mathcal{D}|}},

(107)

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
. Combining (
105
), (
106
) and (
107
) we deduce that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)\leq\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}|\widehat{R}_{n}(A)-R(A)|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert}{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{2n}}+\sqrt{\frac{4\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{4\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{\frac{\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\eqqcolon r_{n}.

(108)

We now work on the event
\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\bigl\{d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)\leq r_{n}\bigr\}
, which occurs with probability at least
1-\delta
by (
108
).
If
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies
d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)>2r_{n}
, then on the event
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta)\bigr)\geq d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)-d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)>r_{n}\geq d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\widehat{R}_{n},\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr),

so
\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}\neq\theta
. Therefore, with
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
as defined in Lemma
24
and
b\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\log\bigl(1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)
, we deduce that on
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\sup\bigl\{|\theta-\theta_{0}|:\theta\in\mathbb{R},\,d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)\leq 2r_{n}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup\bigl\{a\geq 0:f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\leq 2r_{n}\bigr\}=2\inf\bigl\{a\geq 0:f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq 2r_{n}\bigr\},

(109)

where the second inequality follows since by Lemma
24
,
d_{\mathrm{K}}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(\theta),\mathcal{R}(\theta_{0})\bigr)\geq f_{\mathrm{K},b}\bigl(\frac{|\theta-\theta_{0}|}{2}\bigr)
, and the final equality follows since
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is a strictly increasing and continuous function.

When
b\leq 1/2
, we have by (
109
) and Lemma
24
that on
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf\bigl\{a\geq 0:f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq 2r_{n}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf\biggl\{a\geq 0:q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\frac{a}{\sigma}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{\sigma b}{a}\biggr)\geq 6\sqrt{\frac{\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=2\sigma\inf\Biggl\{a\geq 0:a\cdot\phi\biggl(a+\frac{b}{a}\biggr)\geq\sqrt{\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}\Biggr\}.

(110)

Now suppose further that
b\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}
. The assumption on
\delta
means that
b\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}\leq 1/80
and thus
1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}<5/4
. Let
a\coloneqq 20\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}
, so that
a\leq 1/4
. Moreover,
b/a\leq 1/20
, so
a+b/a\leq 3/10
. Therefore,

\displaystyle a\cdot\phi(a+b/a)\geq 20\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}\cdot\phi(3/10)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{5}{4}\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}.

Hence, by (
110
), we have on
\mathcal{E}
that
|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|\leq 40\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}
when
b\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}
.

Next, we consider the case
\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}<b\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))\log(4/\delta)}{(nq(1-\epsilon))^{31/36}}}
. Then
b\leq 1/40
and we again have
1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}<5/4
. Let
a\coloneqq 20b
, so that
a\leq 1/2
. Then

\displaystyle a\cdot\phi(a+b/a)>20\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{20}\biggr)
