\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{5}{4}\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}.

Hence, by (
110
), we have on
\mathcal{E}
that
|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|\leq 40\sigma b
when
\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}}<b\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))\log(4/\delta)}{(nq(1-\epsilon))^{31/36}}}
.

As our third case, suppose that
2\sqrt{\frac{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))\log(4/\delta)}{\{nq(1-\epsilon)\}^{31/36}}}<b\leq 1/2
. Let
a\coloneqq\frac{9b}{\sqrt{\frac{5}{36}\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}}
, so that
a\geq 18\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{\frac{5}{36}(nq(1-\epsilon))^{31/36}}}
. By the assumption (
71
), we have
b\leq\frac{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{432}
, so
a\leq 7b/a
. Therefore,

\displaystyle a\cdot\phi(a+b/a)

\displaystyle\geq 18\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{\frac{5}{36}\bigl\{nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr\}^{31/36}}}\cdot\phi(8b/a)

\displaystyle=18\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{\frac{5}{36}\bigl\{nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr\}^{31/36}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\exp\biggl\{-\frac{40\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}{729}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{5832\log(4/\delta)}{5\pi\bigl\{nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr\}^{2831/2916}}}\geq\sqrt{\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\cdot\frac{36\log(4/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}},

where the final inequality holds since
b\leq\frac{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{432}
.
Hence, by (
110
), we have on
\mathcal{E}
that
|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|\leq\frac{108\sigma b}{\sqrt{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}}
when
2\sqrt{\frac{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))\log(4/\delta)}{(nq(1-\epsilon))^{31/36}}}<b\leq 1/2
.

Finally, consider the case where
1/2<b\leq\frac{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{432}
(when this interval is not vacuous). Then by (
109
) and Lemma
24
we have that on
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf\bigl\{a\geq 0:f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a)\geq 2r_{n}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf\biggl\{a\geq 0:q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\geq\sqrt{\frac{36\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\biggr\}.

(111)

Letting
a\coloneqq\frac{6\sigma b}{\sqrt{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}}
, we have

\displaystyle q(1

\displaystyle-\epsilon)\cdot\Phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\cdot\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{\bigl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\bigr)+\bigl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\bigr)^{-1}}\cdot\phi\biggl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)-\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\biggl\{q(1-\epsilon)\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}}+2b\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}}-2b\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\biggl(\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\biggr)^{-1}\frac{4\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{2}{\sqrt{\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}}\cdot\frac{4\epsilon}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)^{-5/72}

\displaystyle{\geq}\sqrt{\frac{36\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\log(4/\delta)}{n}}=2r_{n}.

Here,
(i)
follows from the Mills ratio bound
\phi(x)/(x+x^{-1})\leq\Phi(-x)\leq\phi(x)/x
for
x>0
;
(ii)
follows since
1/2<b\leq\frac{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{36}
implies
\bigl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\bigr)+\bigl(-\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}\bigr)^{-1}\leq\frac{a}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a}
;
(iii)
follows by substituting the definition of
b
;
(iv)
follows since, by assumption
b\leq\frac{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{432}
, so
\frac{a}{\sigma}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}}{6}
; and
(v)
follows from the assumptions that
b>1/2
, so
q(1-\epsilon)<3\epsilon
, and moreover
\delta\geq 4\exp\bigl(-\frac{\{nq(1-\epsilon)\}^{31/36}}{6400\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}\bigr)
. Hence, by (
111
), we have on
\mathcal{E}
that
|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}|\leq\frac{12\sigma b}{\sqrt{\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}}
when
1/2<b\leq\frac{5\log(nq(1-\epsilon))}{432}
. Combining all four cases yields the desired result.
∎

D.1.4
Proof of Theorem
8

Lemma 25
.

Let
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
\pi\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\{\emptyset,[d]\}\bigr)
,
P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
R\in\mathcal{R}_{\emptyset,[d]}(P,\epsilon,\pi)
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
X\sim P
,
Z\sim R
and define
Z^{(v)}\coloneqq v^{\top}Z\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}}+\star\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z\notin\mathbb{R}^{d}\}}
for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then, writing
P^{(v)}\coloneqq\mathsf{Law}(v^{\top}X)
and
R^{(v)}\coloneqq\mathsf{Law}(Z^{(v)})
, we have
R^{(v)}\in\mathcal{R}\bigl(P^{(v)},\epsilon,\pi([d])\bigr)
.

Proof.

Let
q\coloneqq\pi([d])
. We have
\mathsf{Law}(Z)=(1-\epsilon)\mathsf{Law}(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(1)})+\epsilon\mathsf{Law}(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(2)})
where
\Omega^{(1)}\perp\!\!\!\perp X
and
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(1)}=\bm{1}_{[d]})=q=1-\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(1)}=0)
and where
\Omega^{(2)}
takes values in
\{0,\bm{1}_{[d]}\}
. By properties of disintegrations (see Section
G
), we may define
m^{(v)}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
by
m^{(v)}(y)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{(2)}=\bm{1}_{[d]}\,|\,v^{\top}X=y)
. We also let
\mu^{(v)}
be a
\sigma
-finite measure on
\mathbb{R}
such that
P^{(v)}\ll\mu^{(v)}
and let
p^{(v)}\coloneqq\mathrm{d}P^{(v)}/\mathrm{d}\mu^{(v)}
. Finally, define
g:\mathbb{R}_{\star}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
by

g(z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}q(1-\epsilon)p^{(v)}(z)+\epsilon m^{(v)}(z)p^{(v)}(z)&\text{ if }z\in\mathbb{R}\\
1-q(1-\epsilon)-\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m^{(v)}(y)p^{(v)}(y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{(v)}(y)&\text{ if }z=\star.\end{cases}

Then, for
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{A}g(z)\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{(v)}_{\star}(z)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(v^{\top}X\in A)+\epsilon\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\Omega^{(2)}=\bm{1}_{[d]}\}\cap\{v^{\top}X\in A\}\bigr)

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon)\mathbb{P}\bigl(v^{\top}(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(1)})\in A\bigr)+\epsilon\mathbb{P}\bigl(v^{\top}(X\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega^{(2)})\in A\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(Z^{(v)}\in A)=R^{(v)}(A).

It follows that
R^{(v)}\ll\mu^{(v)}_{\star}
, with Radon–Nikodym derivative
g
. Hence, by Proposition
2
, we have
R^{(v)}\in\mathcal{R}(P^{(v)},\epsilon,q)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8
.

We have
\mathsf{Law}(Z_{1})=(1-\epsilon)\mathsf{Law}(X_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{1}^{(1)})+\epsilon\mathsf{Law}(X_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\star$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.77778}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\star$\cr}}}}\Omega_{1}^{(2)})
where
X_{1}\sim\mathsf{N}_{d}(\theta_{0},\Sigma)
,
\Omega_{1}^{(1)}\perp\!\!\!\perp X_{1}
and
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1}^{(1)}=\bm{1}_{[d]})=q
. Define
m:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
by
m(y)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1}^{(2)}=\bm{1}_{[d]}\,|\,v^{\top}X_{1}=y)
. We claim that the distribution
R^{(v)}
of
Z_{1}^{(v)}

is absolutely continuous with respect to
\lambda_{\star}
, with Radon–Nikodym derivative

\frac{\mathrm{d}R^{(v)}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\star}}(z)=\begin{cases}q(1-\epsilon)\phi_{(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v)}(z)+\epsilon m(z)\phi_{(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v)}(z)&\text{ if }z\in\mathbb{R}\\
1-q(1-\epsilon)-\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(y)\phi_{(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v)}(y)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda(y)&\text{ if }z=\star.\end{cases}

To see this, it suffices to observe that for
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

\int_{A}\frac{\mathrm{d}R^{(v)}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\star}}(z)\,\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\star}(z)=q(1-\epsilon)\mathbb{P}(v^{\top}X_{1}\in A)+\epsilon\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\Omega_{1}^{(2)}=\bm{1}_{[d]}\}\cap\{v^{\top}X_{1}\in A\}\bigr)=\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}^{(v)}\in A).

The claim therefore follows, so by Proposition
2
we have
R^{(v)}\in\mathcal{R}\bigl(\mathsf{N}(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v),\epsilon,q\bigr)
. We deduce from Theorem
7
and a union bound that

\displaystyle\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)-v^{\top}\theta_{0}\bigr)^{2}\lesssim C_{n,q,\epsilon,\delta/(4\cdot 9^{d})}\biggl\{\frac{\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl(d+\log(4/\delta)\bigr)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\frac{\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}\biggr\},

(112)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Next, since any
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
can be written as
v=v_{1}+v_{2}
, where
v_{1}\in\mathcal{N}
and
\|v_{2}\|_{2}\leq 1/4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|v^{\top}\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-v^{\top}\theta_{0}|\leq\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}|v^{\top}\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-v^{\top}\theta_{0}|+\frac{1}{4}\cdot\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\theta_{0}\|_{2},

so

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}\leq\frac{4}{3}\cdot\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}|v^{\top}\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-v^{\top}\theta_{0}|.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}\,\bigl(v^{\top}\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)+\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)-v^{\top}\theta_{0}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}\,\bigl(v^{\top}\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)\bigr)^{2}+4\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}\,\big(v^{\top}\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\max_{v\in\mathcal{N}}\,\big(v^{\top}\theta_{0}-\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}(v)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{n,q,\epsilon,\delta/(4\cdot 9^{d})}\biggl\{\frac{\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl(d+\log(4/\delta)\bigr)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\frac{\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{4\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)}\biggr\},

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where the third inequality follows from the definition of
\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{MK}}
, and the last inequality follows from (
112
).
∎

D.1.5
Proof of Lemma
9

Proof of Lemma
9
.

We first show that, for any
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}(\widehat{R}_{n},R)=\max_{i\in\{0\}\cup[m]}\;\Bigl\{\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i)})\bigr)\Bigr|\vee\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i+1)})\bigr)\Bigr|\Bigr\}.

To this end, fix
i\in\{0\}\cup[m]
. Then, since
\widehat{R}_{n}\big((-\infty,t)\bigr)=i/n
for
t\in[Z_{(i)},Z_{(i+1)})\cap\mathbb{R}
,
t\mapsto R\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)
is increasing on this interval and since
R\ll\lambda_{\star}
by Proposition
2
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[Z_{(i)},Z_{(i+1)})\cap\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\widehat{R}_{n}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)-R\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}\!-\!R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i)})\bigr)\Bigr|\vee\lim_{t\nearrow Z_{(i+1)}}\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}\!-\!R\bigl((-\infty,t)\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i)})\bigr)\Bigr|\vee\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i+1)})\bigr)\Bigr|.

Hence

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\widehat{R}_{n}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)-R\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\bigr|=\max_{i\in\{0\}\cup[m]}\Bigl\{\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i)})\bigr)\Bigr|\vee\Bigl|\frac{i}{n}-R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i+1)})\bigr)\Bigr|\Bigr\}.

Now, by Proposition
2
, for
0\leq V_{1}\leq\ldots\leq V_{m+1}\leq 1
, there exists
R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
such that
V_{i}=R\bigl((-\infty,Z_{(i)}]\bigr)
for
i\in[m]
and
V_{m+1}=R\bigl((-\infty,\infty)\bigr)
if and only if
(V_{1},\ldots,V_{m+1})^{\top}\in\mathcal{V}
. The claim then follows.
∎

D.2
Proofs from Section
4.2.1

D.2.1
Proof of Theorem
10

The proof of Theorem
10
relies on the following preliminary result, which controls the bias.

Proposition 26
.

Let
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
q\in(0,1]
and
\sigma>0
.

(a)

Let
r\geq 2
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{r}}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
and
Z\sim R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
. Then

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\neq\star)-\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\cdot\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}.

(b)

Let
r\geq 1
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
and
Z\sim R\in\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
. Then

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\neq\star)-\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\cdot\biggl\{4\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\;\wedge\;\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Proof.

Let
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
. By translation invariance, we may assume without loss of generality that
\theta_{0}=0
throughout the proof.

(a)
Let
\mu
be a measure on
\mathbb{R}
such that
P\ll\mu
and let
p\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}\mu}
, then by Proposition
2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}R}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\star}}(z)=\begin{cases}q(1-\epsilon)\cdot p(z)+\epsilon\cdot m(z)p(z)\quad&\text{if }z\in\mathbb{R}\\
1-q(1-\epsilon)-\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)&\text{if }z=\star,\end{cases}

(113)

for some Borel measurable function
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\neq\star)\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl|q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}xp(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)+\epsilon\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}xm(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\bigr|}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon\cdot\bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\{Xm(X)\}\bigr|}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq\frac{\epsilon\cdot\sigma\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}},

where the second equality follows from the assumption that
\theta_{0}=0
, and where the inequality follows from Hölder’s inequality and the fact that
\mathbb{E}_{P}(|X|^{r})^{1/r}\leq\sigma
. On the one hand, since
\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}\leq 1
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq\kappa.

(114)

On the other hand, since
m(x)\in[0,1]
, we have
m^{r/(r-1)}(x)\leq m(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and thus
\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}\leq\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}\eqqcolon t
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq\frac{\epsilon t}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon t^{r/(r-1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sup_{t^{\prime}\geq 0}\frac{\epsilon t^{\prime}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon(t^{\prime})^{r/(r-1)}}\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{\epsilon\cdot\{(r-1)q(1-\epsilon)/\epsilon\}^{1-1/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+(r-1)q(1-\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq(r-1)^{-1/r}\kappa^{1/r}\leq\kappa^{1/r},

(115)

where
(i)
follows from the fact that the function
t^{\prime}\mapsto\frac{\epsilon t^{\prime}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon(t^{\prime})^{r/(r-1)}}
is maximised when
t^{\prime}=\{(r-1)q(1-\epsilon)/\epsilon\}^{1-1/r}
. Combining (
114
) and (
115
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\neq\star)\big|\leq\frac{\epsilon\cdot\sigma\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl(m^{r/(r-1)}(X)\bigr)\bigr\}^{1-1/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq\sigma(\kappa\wedge\kappa^{1/r}),

as desired.

(b)
Let
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
such that
Q\ll P
.
By the variational characterisation of Kullback–Leibler divergence
(e.g.
Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart 2013
, Corollary 4.15)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim Q}\bigl(g(X)\bigr)\leq\mathrm{KL}(Q,P)+\log\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P}\bigl(e^{g(X)}\bigr),

(116)

for all Borel measurable functions
g:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
. Now take
Q
to be the conditional distribution of
Z
given
\{Z\neq\star\}
. Let
\mu
and
p
be as in the proof of
(a)
, so that (
113
) holds for some Borel measurable function
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
.
Therefore, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}\mu}(x)=\frac{q(1-\epsilon)\cdot p(x)+\epsilon\cdot m(x)p(x)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(y)p(y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(y)}.

Hence
Q\ll P
and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}P}(x)\in\biggl[1-\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon},\,1+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr],

(117)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, from which we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(Q,P)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\biggl(\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}P}\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}Q\leq\log(1+\kappa).

(118)

Taking
g(\cdot)=|\cdot|^{r}/\sigma^{r}
and combining (
116
) and (
118
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z|^{r}/\sigma^{r}\,\big|\,Z\neq\star\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\log(1+\kappa)+\log\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P}\bigl\{\exp\bigl(|X|^{r}/\sigma^{r}\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\log(1+\kappa)+\log 2=\log(2+2\kappa),

(119)

where the second inequality follows since
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
and since
\theta_{0}=0
by assumption. Thus,

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\neq\star)\bigr|\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z|\,\big|\,Z\neq\star\bigr)\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z|^{r}\,\big|\,Z\neq\star\bigr)^{1/r}\leq\sigma\log^{1/r}(2+2\kappa),

(120)

where the second inequality follows from the conditional version of Jensen’s inequality and the third inequality follows from (
119
). Moreover, by
Götze, Sambale and Sinulis 2021
, we have
\Var_{X\sim P}(X)^{1/2}\leq 2\bigl(\frac{2}{re}\bigr)^{1/r}\sigma\leq 2\sigma
for
r\geq 1
. Hence
P\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{2}}(0,4\sigma^{2})
, so we can apply part
(a)
of the theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\bigl(Z\,\big|\,Z\neq\star\bigr)\big|\leq 2\sigma\kappa.

(121)

Combining (
120
) and (
121
) proves part
(b)
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
10
.

Let
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
.

(a)
Let
\mu
and
p
be as in the proof of Proposition
26
(a)
, so that (
113
) holds for some Borel measurable function
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
.
On the one hand, since
m(X)\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\Var(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)

\displaystyle=\Var(Z_{1}-\theta_{0}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(Z_{1}-\theta_{0})^{2}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}(x-\theta_{0})^{2}\{q(1-\epsilon)p(x)+\epsilon m(x)p(x)\}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}+\frac{\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{(X-\theta_{0})^{2}m(X)\}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq(1+\kappa)\sigma^{2}.

(122)

On the other hand, for
r>2
, we have by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\Var(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}+\frac{\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{(X-\theta_{0})^{2}m(X)\}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\leq\biggl[1+\frac{\epsilon\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigr(m^{r/(r-2)}(X)\bigl)\bigr\}^{1-2/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\biggr]\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl[1+\frac{\epsilon\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigr(m(X)\bigl)\bigr\}^{1-2/r}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\{m(X)\}}\biggr]\sigma^{2}\leq\sup_{t^{\prime}\geq 0}\biggl(1+\frac{\epsilon t^{\prime}}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon(t^{\prime})^{r/(r-2)}}\biggr)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{1+\frac{2}{r}\biggl(\frac{r-2}{2}\biggr)^{1-2/r}\cdot\kappa^{2/r}\biggr\}\sigma^{2}\leq\biggl\{1+\biggl(\frac{2}{r}\biggr)^{2/r}\kappa^{2/r}\biggr\}\sigma^{2}\leq(1+\kappa^{2/r})\sigma^{2},

(123)

where the equality follows since the supremum is attained when
t^{\prime}=\bigl(\frac{(r-2)q(1-\epsilon)}{2\epsilon}\bigr)^{1-2/r}
. Combining (
122
) and (
123
) yields that for
r\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\Var(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\leq(1+\kappa^{2/r})\sigma^{2}.

(124)

By Lemma
38
(b)
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{D}|\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2\}
has
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq 1-\delta/2
, since
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq 8\log(2/\delta)
. Moreover, writing

\mathcal{E}_{1}\coloneqq\biggl\{\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}|Z_{1}\neq\star)\bigr)^{2}\leq 48e(1+\kappa^{2/r})\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(2e/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\biggr\},

we have by Lemma
48
and (
124
) that
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)\geq 1-\delta/2
for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, so

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigm||\mathcal{D}|\bigr)\bigr\}=\sum_{s=\lceil nq(1-\epsilon)/2\rceil}^{n}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}|=s)

\displaystyle\geq\Bigl(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\Bigr)\sum_{s=\lceil nq(1-\epsilon)/2\rceil}^{n}\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{D}|=s)=\Bigl(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\Bigr)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq\Bigl(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\Bigr)^{2}\geq 1-\delta.

(125)

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have by Proposition
26
(a)
that

\displaystyle\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl\{\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\bigr\}^{2}+2\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)-\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 96e\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(2e/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+96e\kappa^{2/r}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(2e/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+2\sigma^{2}(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r})

\displaystyle\leq 192e\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(2e/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+(12e+2)\sigma^{2}(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r}),

where the final inequality follows by considering separately the cases
\kappa\leq 1
and
\kappa>1
, and in the second case noting that
\frac{\log(2e/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\leq 1/8
by assumption. Combining this with (
D.2.1
) establishes the result.

(b)
Let
\mu
and
p
be as in the proof of Proposition
26
(a)
, so that (
113
) holds for some Borel measurable function
m:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
. Then, for integers
\ell\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z_{1}-\theta_{0}|^{\ell}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr)\bigr\}^{1/\ell}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x-\theta_{0}|^{\ell}\{q(1-\epsilon)p(x)+\epsilon m(x)p(x)\}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}m(x)p(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)}\biggr)^{1/\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\frac{\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P}(|X-\theta_{0}|^{\ell})}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{1/\ell}\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\sqrt{1+\kappa}\cdot\sigma\ell,

where
(i)
is true since
X\sim P\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{1}}(\theta_{0},\sigma^{2})
by Lemma
39
, so
\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P}|X-\theta_{0}|^{\ell}\bigr)^{1/\ell}\lesssim\sigma\ell
by
Vershynin 2018
. Moreover, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
122
),
\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z_{1}-\theta_{0}|\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr)\leq\bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl(|Z_{1}-\theta_{0}|^{2}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr)\bigr\}^{1/2}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{1+\kappa}
. Hence, by
Vershynin 2018
again, conditional on
\{Z_{1}\neq\star\}
, we have
\|Z_{1}-\theta_{0}\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{1+\kappa}
. Then, by
Vershynin 2018
, we have, conditional on
\{Z_{1}\neq\star\}
, that

\displaystyle\bigl\|Z_{1}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\bigr\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{1+\kappa}.

(126)

Recall the definition of the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}
from the proof of
(a)
, and observe that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq 1-\delta/4
by Lemma
38
(b)
. Now, similarly to (
D.2.1
), by Bernstein’s inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, Corollary 2.8.3)
and since
\frac{\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\leq 1/8
, there exists a universal constant
C_{2}>0
such that the event

\mathcal{E}_{2}\coloneqq\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\{Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\}}{|\mathcal{D}|}\biggr)^{2}\leq C_{2}(1+\kappa)\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\biggr\}

satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\delta/2
. Moreover, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, by Proposition
26
(b)
,

\displaystyle\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\biggl(\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\{Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)\}}{|\mathcal{D}|}\biggr)^{2}+\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\neq\star)-\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\kappa)\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\sigma^{2}\biggl(\frac{\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}+\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\sigma^{2}\kappa^{2},

(127)

where the penultimate inequality follows from the inequality
ab\leq\frac{a^{2}+b^{2}}{2}
for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, and the final inequality follows from the assumption
\frac{\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\leq 1/8
.

Next, let
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
Q\ll P
. Then the variational characterisation of
\chi^{2}
-divergence
(e.g.,
Polyanskiy and Wu 2024
, Example 7.4)
yields that

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{X\sim Q}\{g(X)\}\leq 1+\chi^{2}(Q,P)+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P}\{g^{2}(X)\},

(128)

for all Borel measurable
g:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
. We first consider the case
r>1
. Now take
Q
to be the conditional distribution of
Z_{1}
given
\{Z_{1}\neq\star\}
, so that, by the representation in (
117
), we have

\chi^{2}(Q,P)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl(\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}P}-1\biggr)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P\leq\kappa^{2}.

Thus, taking
g:x\mapsto\exp\bigl\{\lambda(x-\theta_{0})\bigr\}
in (
128
) and applying Lemma
40
yields that

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\bigl[\exp\bigl\{\lambda(Z_{1}-\theta_{0})\bigr\}\bigm|Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr]\leq 1+\kappa^{2}+2\exp\bigl\{(2\sigma\lambda)^{r/(r-1)}\bigr\},

for all
\lambda>0
. Hence, for
s\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\exp\biggl(\frac{\lambda}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}(Z_{i}-\theta_{0})\biggr)\,\biggm|\,|\mathcal{D}|=s\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq s\log\Biggl\{\frac{1}{2}\biggl[1+\kappa^{2}+2\exp\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{2\sigma\lambda}{s}\biggr)^{r/(r-1)}\biggr\}\biggr]\Biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq s\biggl\{\log(1+\kappa^{2})+\log 2+\biggl(\frac{2\sigma\lambda}{s}\biggr)^{r/(r-1)}\biggr\},

(129)

where the final inequality follows from the fact that
\log\bigl(\frac{a+b}{2}\bigr)\leq\log a+\log b
for all
a,b\geq 1
.
Then, applying a Chernoff bound gives that for every
t\geq 0
and
s\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\geq t\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}(Z_{i}-\theta_{0})\geq t\biggm||\mathcal{D}|=s\biggr)\leq\exp\bigl(-\psi^{*}(t)\bigr),

where

\displaystyle\psi^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{\lambda>0}\,\biggl\{\lambda t-s\log(1+\kappa^{2})-s\log 2-\frac{(2\sigma\lambda)^{r/(r-1)}}{s^{1/(r-1)}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{st^{r}}{(2\sigma)^{r}}\cdot\biggl(\frac{r-1}{r}\biggr)^{r-1}\cdot\frac{1}{r}-s\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\geq\frac{st^{r}}{(2\sigma)^{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{er}-s\log(2+2\kappa^{2}),

since the supremum over
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
is attained at
\lambda^{*}\coloneqq\bigl(\frac{r-1}{r}\cdot\frac{ts^{1/(r-1)}}{(2\sigma)^{r/(r-1)}}\bigr)^{r-1}
.
By replacing
Z_{i}-\theta_{0}
with
-(Z_{i}-\theta_{0})
for
i\in[n]
, we deduce that for every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}|\geq t\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)\leq 2\exp\biggl\{-\frac{st^{r}}{(2\sigma)^{r}}\cdot\frac{1}{er}+s\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\biggr\}.

Hence, defining the event

\mathcal{E}_{3}\coloneqq\biggl\{(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})^{2}\leq\biggl(\frac{(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)/2}+(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\},

and proceeding in a similar fashion to (
D.2.1
), we deduce that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3})\geq 1-\delta/2
. Moreover, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\frac{(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)/2}+(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\biggr\}^{2/r}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\frac{(2\sigma)^{r}er}{4}+(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\biggr\}^{2/r}\leq\biggl\{\frac{3}{2}\cdot(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\biggr\}^{2/r}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\{3\cdot(2\sigma)^{r}er\log(2+2\kappa)\bigr\}^{2/r}\leq(9e\sigma)^{2}\log^{2/r}(2+2\kappa).

(130)

Thus, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
, which satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3})\geq 1-\delta
, we combine (
127
) and (
130
) to obtain the desired result for
r>1
.

Finally, we consider the case where
r=1
. By
Zhivotovskiy 2024
, (
128
) yields, with the same choice of
Q
and
g
, that

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\bigl[\exp\bigl\{\lambda(Z_{1}-\theta_{0})\bigr\}\bigm|Z_{1}\neq\star\bigr]\leq 1+\kappa^{2}+\exp\bigl\{(2\sigma\lambda)^{2}\bigr\},

for all
|\lambda|\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma}
. Hence, by a similar argument to the
r>1
case,

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\exp\biggl(\frac{\lambda}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}(Z_{i}-\theta_{0})\biggr)\,\biggm|\,|\mathcal{D}|=s\biggr\}\leq s\biggl\{\log(1+\kappa^{2})+\biggl(\frac{2\sigma\lambda}{s}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\},

(131)

for
s\in[n]
and
|\lambda|\leq\frac{s}{2\sigma}
. Then, applying a Chernoff bound yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}|\geq t\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\Bigl|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}(Z_{i}-\theta_{0})\Bigr|\geq t\biggm||\mathcal{D}|=s\biggr)\leq 2\exp\bigl(-\psi^{*}(t)\bigr),

where

\displaystyle\psi^{*}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{0<\lambda\leq\frac{s}{2\sigma}}\,\biggl\{\lambda t-s\log(1+\kappa^{2})-\frac{(2\sigma\lambda)^{2}}{s}\biggr\}.

Taking
t\coloneqq 8\sigma\log(2+2\kappa^{2})
and
\lambda=s/(2\sigma)
yields, for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}|\geq t\bigm||\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\bigl\{-3s\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+s\bigr\}\leq 2\exp(-s)\leq\frac{\delta}{4},

where the final inequality follows from the assumption
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq 8\log(8/\delta)
. Therefore, letting
\mathcal{E}_{4}\coloneqq\bigl\{|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}|<8\sigma\log(2+2\kappa^{2})\bigr\}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4})\geq 1-\delta/2
. Moreover, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
,

\displaystyle(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0})^{2}<64\sigma^{2}\log^{2}(2+2\kappa^{2})\leq 256\sigma^{2}\log^{2}(2+2\kappa).

(132)

Thus, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, which satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4})\geq 1-\delta
, we combine (
127
) and (
132
) to obtain the desired result for
r=1
.
∎

D.2.2
Proof of Theorem
11

For
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
K>0
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{b}}(\theta,K)\coloneqq\Bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}):\mathbb{E}_{P}(X)=\theta,\text{$P$ is supported on an interval of length at most $K$}\Bigr\}.

(133)

Proof of Theorem
11
.

(a)
Define
a\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\in(0,1]
and
b\coloneqq\frac{\sigma}{2}\cdot a^{-1/r}>0
. Let
X_{1}\sim P_{1}
and
X_{2}\sim P_{2}
be random variables satisfying

\displaystyle X_{1}=\begin{cases}-b\quad&\text{with probability }\frac{1}{a+1}\\
b&\text{with probability }\frac{a}{a+1}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad X_{2}=\begin{cases}-b\quad&\text{with probability }\frac{a}{a+1}\\
b&\text{with probability }\frac{1}{a+1}.\end{cases}

Then
\theta_{1}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}(X_{1})=-\frac{(1-a)b}{a+1}
and
\theta_{2}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}(X_{2})=\frac{(1-a)b}{a+1}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(|X_{1}-\theta_{1}|^{r}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2ab)^{r}+a(2b)^{r}}{(a+1)^{r+1}}\leq a\cdot(2b)^{r}=\sigma^{r},

where we have used the fact that
a^{r}+a\leq a(a+1)\leq a(a+1)^{r+1}
; by symmetry,
\mathbb{E}\bigl(|X_{2}-\theta_{2}|^{r}\bigr)\leq\sigma^{r}
. Consequently,
P_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{r}}(\theta_{1},\sigma^{2})
and
P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{r}}(\theta_{2},\sigma^{2})
. Now define
R_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
by

\displaystyle R_{0}(\{-b\})\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{a+1}\eqqcolon R_{0}(\{b\})\quad\text{ and }\quad R_{0}(\{\star\})\coloneqq 1-R_{0}(\{-b\})-R_{0}(\{b\})\in[0,1).

By Proposition
2
,
R_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(P_{1},\epsilon,q)\cap\mathcal{R}(P_{2},\epsilon,q)
. Therefore, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})^{2}}{4}=\biggl\{\frac{(1-a)b}{a+1}\biggr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4}\cdot\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\biggr)^{2}\biggl(\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2/r}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{36}\cdot\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\},

where the final bound is obtained by considering separately the cases
\epsilon\leq q(1-\epsilon)
and
\epsilon>q(1-\epsilon)
. This proves the second term in the lower bound.

For the first term in the lower bound, we observe that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{b}}(\theta,\sigma)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{L^{r}}(\theta,\sigma^{2})
for all
r\geq 2
. We therefore obtain the desired conclusion by choosing the contamination distribution
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
such that
Q\bigl(\{\star\}\bigr)=1
and applying Proposition
46
(b)
.

(b)
Define
P_{1},P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
with Lebesgue densities
p_{1},p_{2}
respectively as in Lemma
27
, so that
P_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}(X_{1}),\sigma^{2}\bigr)
and
P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}(X_{2}),\sigma^{2}\bigr)
. Further, with
b>0
defined as in Lemma
27
, define
R_{1}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
through its Radon–Nikodym derivative

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}R_{1}}{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\star}}(z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}q(1-\epsilon)\cdot p_{1}(z)\quad&\text{if }z\in(-\infty,b)\\
\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot p_{1}(z)\quad&\text{if }z\in[b,\infty)\\
1-q(1-\epsilon)\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{b}p_{1}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x-\bigl\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\bigr\}\cdot\int_{b}^{\infty}p_{1}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\quad&\text{if }z=\star,\end{cases}

so that, by Proposition
2
,
R_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(P_{1},\epsilon,q)\cap\mathcal{R}(P_{2},\epsilon,q)
. Therefore, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}(X_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}(X_{1})\bigr\}^{2}}{4}.

(134)

Now, writing
\sigma_{0}\coloneqq\sigma/C_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}(X_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}(X_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\int_{b}^{\infty}xp_{1}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\int_{0}^{b}xp_{1}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle{=}\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggl\{be^{-(b/\sigma_{0})^{r}}+\int_{b}^{\infty}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x\biggr\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\biggl\{-be^{-(b/\sigma_{0})^{r}}+\int_{0}^{b}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x\biggr\}

\displaystyle{=}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\biggr)\cdot\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot\sigma_{0}\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\int_{b}^{\infty}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\int_{0}^{b}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x,

(135)

where
(i)
follows from integration by parts,
(ii)
follows by substituting the definition of
b
.
Now let
h(t)\coloneqq\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x
. Then

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(t)=\frac{te^{-(t/\sigma_{0})^{r}}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(x/\sigma_{0})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x}{t^{2}}\leq 0,

so
h
is a decreasing function.

First consider the case where
\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\geq e^{2^{r}}-2
or equivalently
\epsilon\geq\frac{\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}{1+\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}
, so that
\log^{1/r}\bigl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)\geq 2
and

\displaystyle h(b)\leq h(2\sigma_{0})=\frac{\int_{0}^{2}e^{-x^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x}{2}=\frac{\int_{0}^{1}e^{-x^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{1}^{2}e^{-x^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}x}{2}\leq\frac{1+e^{-1}}{2}.

Hence, by (
135
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{2}}(X_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{P_{1}}(X_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\biggr)\cdot\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot\sigma_{0}\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot\frac{1+e^{-1}}{2}\cdot\sigma_{0}\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-1}}{2}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot\sigma_{0}\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-e^{-1}}{8}\cdot\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}\cdot\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr).

Therefore, by (
134
), when
\epsilon\geq\frac{\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}{1+\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr).

(136)

Next consider the case where
\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\leq 1
, or equivalently
\epsilon\leq q/(1+q)
. Define
P_{3},P_{4}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
by

\displaystyle P_{3}\biggl(\biggl\{-\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr)\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\eqqcolon P_{4}\biggl(\biggl\{\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr),\;P_{3}\biggl(\biggl\{\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr)\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\eqqcolon P_{4}\biggl(\biggl\{-\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr).

Thus
P_{3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{P_{3}}(X_{3}),\sigma^{2}\bigr)
and
P_{4}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{P_{4}}(X_{4}),\sigma^{2}\bigr)
. Further define
R_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
by

\displaystyle R_{2}\biggl(\biggl\{-\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\eqqcolon R_{2}\biggl(\biggl\{\frac{\sigma}{4}\biggr\}\biggr),

\displaystyle R_{2}(\{\star\})

\displaystyle\coloneqq 1-\frac{2q(1-\epsilon)\{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon\}}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}.

By Proposition
2
,
R_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(P_{3},\epsilon,q)\cap\mathcal{R}(P_{4},\epsilon,q)
. Therefore, by
Ma, Verchand and Samworth 2024
, when
\epsilon\leq\frac{q}{1+q}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\geq\frac{\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{3}}(X_{3})-\mathbb{E}_{P_{4}}(X_{4})\bigr\}^{2}}{4}\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{16}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\biggr)^{2}\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{144}\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}.

(137)

Combining (
136
) and (
137
) yields that when
\epsilon\leq\frac{q}{1+q}
or
\epsilon\geq\frac{\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}{1+\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\cdot\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\}.

(138)

Further observe that
\mathcal{R}(P,\frac{q}{1+q},q)\subseteq\mathcal{R}(P,\epsilon,q)
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
when
\epsilon>\frac{q}{1+q}
. Thus, by (
138
), we deduce that when
\frac{q}{1+q}<\epsilon<\frac{\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}{1+\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\delta,\mathcal{P}_{\Theta},|\cdot|^{2})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\cdot\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\frac{q}{1+q}}{q(1-\frac{q}{1+q})}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\frac{2q}{1+q}}{q(1-\frac{q}{1+q})}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\cdot\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\},

(139)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that when
\epsilon<\frac{\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}{1+\{\exp(2^{r})-2\}q}
,

\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)<\log^{2/r}\bigl(2+2(e^{2^{r}}-2)\bigr)\leq\log^{2/r}\bigl(e^{2^{r}+\log 2}\bigr)\leq(2\cdot 2^{r})^{2/r}=2^{2/r}\cdot 2\leq 8.

Combining (
138
) and (
139
) yields the second term in the lower bound.

For the first term in the lower bound, we observe that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{b}}(\theta,\sigma/2)\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}(\theta,\sigma^{2})
for all
r\geq 1
. We therefore obtain the desired conclusion by choosing the contamination distribution
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\star})
such that
Q\bigl(\{\star\}\bigr)=1
and applying Proposition
46
(b)
.
∎

Lemma 27
.

Let
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
q\in(0,1]
,
\sigma>0
and
r\geq 1
. There exists a universal constant
C_{0}>0
such that if
X_{1}\sim P_{1}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
and
X_{2}\sim P_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
have Lebesgue densities
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
respectively, where
p_{1}(x)\coloneqq\frac{rx^{r-1}}{(\sigma/C_{0})^{r}}e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\geq 0\}}
and

\displaystyle p_{2}(x)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot p_{1}(x)\quad&\text{if }x<b\\
\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\cdot p_{1}(x)\quad&\text{if }x\geq b\end{cases}\quad\text{with}\quad b\coloneqq\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}\log^{1/r}\biggl(2+\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr),

then
\|X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{1}\|_{\psi_{r}}\vee\|X_{2}-\mathbb{E}X_{2}\|_{\psi_{r}}\leq\sigma
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{P}(|X_{1}|\geq x)=e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}
for all
x\geq 0
, we have by
Vershynin 2018
that
\|X_{1}^{r}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{1}(\sigma/C_{0})^{r}
for some universal constant
C_{1}>0
, so
\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{r}}\leq C_{1}^{1/r}\sigma/C_{0}\leq(C_{1}\vee 1)\sigma/C_{0}
. Then, by
Götze, Sambale and Sinulis 2021
, we have

\|X_{1}-\mathbb{E}X_{1}\|_{\psi_{r}}\leq\biggl\{1+\biggl(\frac{2}{(re)^{1/r}\log 2}\biggr)^{1/r}\biggr\}(C_{1}\vee 1)\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}\leq 4(C_{1}\vee 1)\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}.

Turning to
X_{2}
, first observe that
p_{2}
is a Lebesgue density, since

\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{2}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\{1-e^{-(C_{0}b/\sigma)^{r}}\}+\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}e^{-(C_{0}b/\sigma)^{r}}=1.

Now, for
x\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{2}-b\geq x)=\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\cdot\mathbb{P}(X_{1}\geq b+x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\cdot e^{-(C_{0}b/\sigma)^{r}-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}

\displaystyle=\frac{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}{2q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\cdot e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}\leq e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}.

Define
a\coloneqq\frac{q(1-\epsilon)}{q(1-\epsilon)+\epsilon}\in(0,1]
, so that
b=(\sigma/C_{0})\log^{1/r}\bigl(\frac{1+a}{a}\bigr)
.
For
x\in[0,b]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{2}-b\leq-x)

\displaystyle=a\cdot\mathbb{P}(X_{1}\leq b-x)\leq a\leq\frac{2a}{1+a}=2e^{-(C_{0}b/\sigma)^{r}}\leq 2e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}.

For
x>b
, we have
\mathbb{P}(X_{2}-b\leq-x)=0
.
Combining these inequalities, we obtain
\mathbb{P}(|X_{2}-b|\geq x)\leq 3e^{-(C_{0}x/\sigma)^{r}}
for all
x\geq 0
. Therefore, by
Vershynin 2018
, we deduce
7
7

7

Note that in
Vershynin 2018
, the condition is that
\mathbb{P}(|X|\geq t)\leq 2\exp(-t/K_{1})
for all
t\geq 0
. However, the result is still true if we replace the factor
2
by
3
. See for example,
Vershynin 2018
for the proof strategy.
that
\|(X_{2}-b)^{r}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{2}(\sigma/C_{0})^{r}
for some universal constant
C_{2}>0
, so

\|X_{2}-b\|_{\psi_{r}}\leq C_{2}^{1/r}\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}\leq(C_{2}\vee 1)\frac{\sigma}{C_{0}}.

By
Götze, Sambale and Sinulis 2021
again,
\|X_{2}-\mathbb{E}X_{2}\|_{\psi_{r}}\leq 4(C_{2}\vee 1)\sigma/C_{0}
. Finally, taking
C_{0}\coloneqq 4C_{1}\vee 4C_{2}\vee 4
completes the proof.
∎

D.2.3
Proof of Theorem
12

The following proposition, which is analogous to Proposition
26
in the univariate case, will be used in the proof of Theorem
12
.

Proposition 28
.

Let
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\Sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{++}^{d\times d}
,
\epsilon\in[0,1)
,
\delta\in(0,1]
,
\pi\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\{\emptyset,[d]\}\bigr)
and
q\coloneqq\pi([d])
.

(a)

Let
r\geq 2
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{d,L^{r}}(\theta_{0},\Sigma)
and
Z\sim R\in\mathcal{R}_{\emptyset,[d]}(P,\epsilon,\pi)
. Then

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\wedge\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}.

(b)

Let
r\geq 1
,
P\in\mathcal{P}_{d,\psi_{r}}(\theta_{0},\Sigma)
, and
Z\sim R\in\mathcal{R}_{\emptyset,[d]}(P,\epsilon,\pi)
. Then

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\biggl\{4\biggl(\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)^{2}\;\wedge\;\log^{2/r}\biggl(2+\frac{2\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Proof.

Let
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
,
X\sim P
,
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
Z^{(v)}\coloneqq v^{\top}Z\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}}+\star\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z\notin\mathbb{R}^{d}\}}
,
R^{(v)}\coloneqq\mathsf{Law}(Z^{(v)})
and
P^{(v)}\coloneqq\mathsf{Law}(v^{\top}X)
. By Lemma
25
, we have
R^{(v)}\in\mathcal{R}(P^{(v)},\epsilon,q)
.

(a)
Since
P^{(v)}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{r}}(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v)
we have by Proposition
26
(a)
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(Z\,|\,Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\bigl\{v^{\top}\mathbb{E}(Z|Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-v^{\top}\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Z^{(v)}|Z^{(v)}\neq\star)-v^{\top}\theta_{0}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\Sigma v\cdot(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r})

\displaystyle=\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r}),

as required.

(b)
We now have
P^{(v)}\in\mathcal{P}_{\psi_{r}}(v^{\top}\theta_{0},v^{\top}\Sigma v)
, so the proof is the same as part
(a)
, except that we use Proposition
26
(b)
instead.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12
.

Let
\kappa\coloneqq\frac{\epsilon}{q(1-\epsilon)}
.

(a)
For
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have by the same argument as the proof of (
124
) that

\displaystyle\Var(v^{\top}Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\leq(1+\kappa^{2/r})v^{\top}\Sigma v.

Therefore, writing
\Gamma\coloneqq\Cov(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\in\mathcal{S}_{+}^{d\times d}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+\kappa^{2/r})\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\quad\text{and}\quad\tr(\Gamma)\leq(1+\kappa^{2/r})\tr(\Sigma).

(140)

By Lemma
38
(b)
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{D}|\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2\}
has
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq 1-\delta/2
, since
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq 8\log(2/\delta)
. Moreover, writing

\mathcal{E}_{1}\coloneqq\biggl\{\bigl\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}\cdot\frac{\tr(\Gamma)+\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(2/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\biggr\},

we have by
Depersin and Lecué 2022b
that when
C_{1}>0
is a sufficiently large universal constant, we have
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{1}\,\big|\,|\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)\geq 1-\delta/2
for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, so
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1})\geq 1-\delta
. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have by (
140
) and Proposition
28
(a)
that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}+2\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tr(\Sigma)+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(2/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)+\log(2/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\cdot\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\kappa^{2/r}+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tr(\Sigma)+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(2/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}(\kappa^{2}\wedge\kappa^{2/r}),

where the final inequality follows by considering separately the cases
\kappa\leq 1
and
\kappa>1
, and in the second case noting that
\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)\leq Cnq(1-\epsilon)
and
\log(2/\delta)\leq nq(1-\epsilon)/C
.

(b)
For
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have by the same argument as in the proof of (
126
) that conditional on
\{Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|v^{\top}Z_{1}-\mathbb{E}(v^{\top}Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\sqrt{(1+\kappa)v^{\top}\Sigma v},

so that
Z_{1}\,|\,\{Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\in\mathcal{P}_{d,\psi_{1}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}(v^{\top}Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}),(1+\kappa)\Sigma\bigr)
. By Lemma
38
(b)
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{D}|\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2\}
satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq 1-\delta/4
since
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq 8\log(8/\delta)
. Moreover, writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}\coloneqq\biggl\{\bigl\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\leq 48(1+\kappa)\cdot\frac{\tr(\Sigma)+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}\biggr\},

we have by Lemma
42
(a consequence of the PAC–Bayes lemma) that
\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{2}\,\big|\,|\mathcal{D}|=s\bigr)\geq 1-\delta/4
for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, so
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})\geq 1-\delta/2
. On
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have by Proposition
28
(b)
that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}+2\bigl\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}\,|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d})-\theta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tr(\Sigma)+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\kappa\cdot\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)+\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\kappa^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\tr(\Sigma)+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log(8/\delta)}{nq(1-\epsilon)}+\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\kappa^{2},

(141)

where the final inequality follows by considering separately the cases
\kappa\leq 1
and
\kappa>1
, and in the second case noting that
\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)\leq nq(1-\epsilon)
and
\log(8/\delta)\leq nq(1-\epsilon)/8
.

For the last term in the upper bound, we first consider the case where
r>1
. For
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have by the same argument as in the proof of (
129
) that

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\exp\bigl(\lambda w^{\top}(Z_{1}-\theta_{0})\bigr)\bigm|Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\bigr\}\leq\log(1+\kappa^{2})+\log 2+\bigl(2\lambda\sqrt{w^{\top}\Sigma w}\bigr)^{r/(r-1)},

(142)

for all
\lambda>0
. Let
\beta\coloneqq\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)
, let
\mu
denote the distribution of
\mathsf{N}_{d}(0,\beta^{-1}\Sigma)
and for
u\in\Sigma^{1/2}\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, let
\rho_{u}
denote the conditional distribution of
Y
given
\bigl\{\|Y-u\|_{2}\leq 2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr\}
, where
Y\sim\mathsf{N}_{d}(u,\beta^{-1}\Sigma)
. By Chebychev’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|Y-u\|_{2}\geq 2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)\leq\frac{\tr(\Sigma)}{4\beta\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}}=\frac{1}{4}.

Hence, by the third displayed equation of
Zhivotovskiy 2024
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\rho_{u},\mu)=\log\biggl(\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|Y-u\|_{2}\leq 2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)}\biggr)+\frac{\beta}{2}\leq 2\log 2+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)}{2}.

Fix
u\in\Sigma^{1/2}\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be such that
\|v-u\|_{2}\leq 2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}
, and for
\lambda>0
, define
f_{\lambda}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
by
f_{\lambda}(x,y)\coloneqq\lambda y^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}(x-\theta_{0})
. Then, since
\|v\|_{2}\leq 3\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}
, we have by (
142
) that

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim R}\bigl(e^{f_{\lambda}(Z,v)}\bigm|Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\bigr)\leq\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+\bigl(6\lambda\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)^{r/(r-1)},

so
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{u}\sim\rho_{u}}\bigl\{\log\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim R}\bigl(e^{f_{\lambda}(Z,\xi_{u})}\bigm|Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\bigr)\bigr\}\leq\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+\bigl(6\lambda\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)^{r/(r-1)}
.
Therefore, for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, by the PAC–Bayes lemma (Lemma
41
), conditional on
|\mathcal{D}|=s
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta/4
that

\displaystyle\biggl\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}Z_{i}-\theta_{0}\biggr\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{u\in\Sigma^{1/2}\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{\lambda|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{u}\sim\rho_{u}}f_{\lambda}(Z_{i},\xi_{u})

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\lambda>0}\biggl\{\frac{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})}{\lambda}+\bigl(6\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)^{r/(r-1)}\lambda^{1/(r-1)}+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)/2+2\log(4/\delta)}{s\lambda}\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\leq}12\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\biggl\{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)/2+2\log(4/\delta)}{s}\biggr\}^{1/r}

\displaystyle{\leq}12\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigl\{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+2\bigr\}^{1/r}\lesssim\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\log^{1/r}(2+2\kappa),

where
(i)
follows by choosing
\lambda=\frac{1}{6\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}}\bigl\{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)/2+2\log(4/\delta)}{s}\bigr\}^{(r-1)/r}
and
(ii)
follows from the assumptions that
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)
and
\delta\geq 8\exp\bigl(-nq(1-\epsilon)/8\bigr)
. Hence, there exists a universal constant
C_{1}>0
such that the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}\coloneqq\biggl\{\biggl\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}Z_{i}-\theta_{0}\biggr\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{1}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log^{2/r}(2+2\kappa)\biggr\},

(143)

satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3})\geq 1-\delta/2
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
, which has probability at least
1-\delta
, we combine (
141
) and (
143
) to obtain the desired result for
r>1
.

Finally, we consider the case where
r=1
. For
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have by the same argument as the proof of (
131
) that

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\exp\bigl(\lambda w^{\top}(Z_{1}-\theta_{0})\bigr)\,\big|\,Z_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\bigr\}\leq\log(1+\kappa^{2})+\log 2+\bigl(2\lambda\sqrt{w^{\top}\Sigma w}\bigr)^{2},

for
|\lambda|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1/2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{w^{\top}\Sigma w}}
. Hence, for
s\geq nq(1-\epsilon)/2
, by following the same proof as the
r>1
case above, we deduce that, conditional on
|\mathcal{D}|=s
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta/4
that

\displaystyle\biggl\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}Z_{i}-\theta_{0}\biggr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\lambda\in(0,\frac{1}{2}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1/2}]}\biggl\{\frac{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})}{\lambda}+\bigl(6\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigr)^{2}\lambda+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)/2+2\log(4/\delta)}{s\lambda}\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\leq}2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\biggl\{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+9+\frac{\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)/2+2\log(4/\delta)}{s}\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\leq}2\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\bigl\{\log(2+2\kappa^{2})+11\bigr\}\lesssim\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{1/2}\log(2+2\kappa),

where
(i)
follows by choosing
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1/2}
, and
(ii)
follows from the assumptions that
nq(1-\epsilon)\geq\mathbf{r}(\Sigma)
and
\delta\geq 8\exp\bigl(-nq(1-\epsilon)/8\bigr)
. Hence, there exists a universal constant
C_{2}>0
such that the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}\coloneqq\biggl\{\biggl\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}}Z_{i}-\theta_{0}\biggr\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{2}\|\Sigma\|_{\mathrm{op}}\log^{2}(2+2\kappa)\biggr\},

(144)

satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4})\geq 1-\delta/2
. Thus, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
, which has probability at least
1-\delta
, we combine (
141
) and (
144
) to obtain the desired result for
r=1
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs from Section
5

E.1
Proof of Lemma
13

Proof of Lemma
13
.

(a)
Let
(v_{m})
be a sequence in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{m}|>\gamma\bigr)\searrow\inf_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v|>\gamma\bigr)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Then by compactness of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists a subsequence
(v_{m_{k}})
, as well as
v_{*}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, for which
v_{m_{k}}\rightarrow v_{*}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. But then
|X_{1}^{\top}v_{m_{k}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}|X_{1}^{\top}v_{*}|
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, so by, e.g.,
van der Vaart 1998
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{*}|>\gamma\bigr)\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{m_{k}}|>\gamma\bigr)=\inf_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v|>\gamma\bigr).

It follows that the infimum in the definition of
\beta
is attained.

If
\beta=0
for all
\gamma>0
, then for every
\gamma>0
we can find
v_{*}(\gamma)\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with
\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{*}(\gamma)|>\gamma\bigr)=0
. Writing
v_{m}\coloneqq v_{*}(1/m)
, there exist integers
1\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\ldots
and
v_{**}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with
v_{m_{k}}\rightarrow v_{**}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Since
|X_{1}^{\top}v_{m_{k}}|-1/m_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}|X_{1}^{\top}v_{**}|
as
k\rightarrow\infty
we have by
van der Vaart 1998
again that

\mathbb{P}\bigl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{**}|>0\bigr)\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\biggl(|X_{1}^{\top}v_{m_{k}}|>\frac{1}{m_{k}}\biggr)=0.

But then, defining the hyperplane
H\coloneqq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:x^{\top}v_{**}=0\}
, we have
P(H)=1
.

(b)
The claim is equivalent to showing that there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that if
\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\leq c\beta^{2}
, then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma\}}\leq-2\beta.

To establish this, let
\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\{x\mapsto-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x^{\top}v|>\gamma\}}:v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma\}}+3\beta

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{-\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma\}}+\mathbb{P}(|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{h(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}h(X_{i})\bigr\}\eqqcolon V,

(145)

where the first inequality follows since
\mathbb{P}(|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma)\geq 3\beta
for all
v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. By the bounded differences inequality
(e.g.,
Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart 2013
, Theorem 6.2)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle V\leq\mathbb{E}(V)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2n}}.

(146)

For a collection
\mathcal{H}_{1}
of binary-valued functions, we write
\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}_{1})
for its Vapnik–Chervonenkis dimension. For
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
, define
g_{v,b}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
g_{v,b}(x)\coloneqq x^{\top}v+b
, and define the vector space
\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\{g_{v,b}:v\in\mathbb{R}^{d},b\in\mathbb{R}\}
. Now let
\mathcal{H^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{x\mapsto-\mathbbm{1}_{\{g(x)>0\}}:g\in\mathcal{G}\}
, which by
Wainwright 2019
satisfies
\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{G})=d+1
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=\bigl\{x\mapsto-\mathbbm{1}_{\{g_{v,-\gamma}(x)>0\}\cup\{g_{-v,-\gamma}(x)>0\}}:v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\bigr\}\subseteq\bigl\{x\mapsto-\mathbbm{1}_{\{g_{1}(x)>0\}\cup\{g_{2}(x)>0\}}:g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}\bigr\}.

Hence, by
Blumer et al. 1989
, we have
\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H})\leq 4\log_{2}(6)\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\leq 11d+11
.
We deduce by
Vershynin 2018
that there exists a universal constant
C_{1}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}(V)\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d+1}{n}}
. Thus, by (
145
) and (
146
) we conclude that there exists a universal constant
C_{2}>0
such that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|X_{i}^{\top}v|>\gamma\}}+3\beta\leq\mathbb{E}(V)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2n}}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\leq\beta,

where the final inequality follows by choosing
c\coloneqq 1/C_{2}^{2}
and using the assumption that
\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\leq c\beta^{2}
. This proves the claim.
∎

E.2
Proof of Theorem
14

We begin with some preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 29
.

Consider the setting of Theorem
14
. There exists a universal constant
C>0
such that for
\delta\in(0,1]
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta
conditional on
X_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,X_{n}=x_{n}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta},R_{n,\theta})\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}.

Proof.

First define

\displaystyle V\coloneqq\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta},R_{n,\theta})=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{sym}}}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z_{i}-x_{i}^{\top}\theta\in A\}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{R}_{i,\theta}(A)\biggr|.

Then, by the bounded differences inequality
(e.g.,
Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart 2013
, Theorem 6.2)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle V\leq\mathbb{E}(V)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2n}}.

(147)

Now define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\Bigl\{g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{\star}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ s.t. }g(x,z)=(z\!-\!x^{\top}\theta\!-\!t)\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\neq\star\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{z=\star\}}\text{ for some }\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,t\in\mathbb{R}\Bigr\},

and define
\mathcal{H}_{+}\coloneqq\bigl\{(x,z)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{\{g(x,z)\leq 0\}}:g\in\mathcal{G}\bigr\}
and
\mathcal{H}_{-}\coloneqq\bigl\{(x,z)\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{\{g(x,z)\leq 0\}}:g\in-\mathcal{G}\bigr\}
. Then

\displaystyle V=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{+}\cup\mathcal{H}_{-}}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{h(x_{i},Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}h(x_{i},Z_{i})\bigr\}\biggr|.

Since
\mathcal{G}
is a vector space of functions with
\dim(\mathcal{G})=d+1
, by
Mohri, Rostamizadeh and Talwalkar 2018
and
Wainwright 2019
, we deduce that
\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}_{+}\cup\mathcal{H}_{-})\leq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}_{+})+\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal{H}_{-})+1\leq 2\dim(\mathcal{G})+1\leq 2d+3
. Therefore, applying
Vershynin 2018
yields that
\mathbb{E}(V)\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{2d+3}{n}}
for some universal constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Combining this with (
147
) proves the desired result.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Consider the setting of Theorem
14
, and assume that
\theta\neq\theta_{0}
. Then, writing
a\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{0}-\theta\|_{2}>0
and
b\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\log\bigl(1+\frac{4(1-\beta q(1-\epsilon))}{\beta q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}\bigl(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}\bigr)\geq\beta q(1-\epsilon)\Phi\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)-\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)\eqqcolon f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a),

where
f_{\mathrm{K},b}:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
is strictly increasing and continuous.

Proof.

By Assumption
1
, we may assume without loss of generality that there exists
\mathcal{T}_{+}\subseteq[n]
such that
|\mathcal{T}_{+}|\geq\beta n
and
-x_{i}^{\top}(\theta_{0}-\theta)\geq 2a\gamma
. By Proposition
2
, for
i\in\mathcal{T}_{+}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{i,\theta}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\geq q(1-\epsilon)\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}\bigl(t-x_{i}^{\top}(\theta_{0}-\theta)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq q(1-\epsilon)\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(t+2a\gamma)

\displaystyle=q(1-\epsilon)\Phi_{(-2a\gamma,\sigma)}(t).

Moreover, by Proposition
2
again for
R_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
R_{0}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\leq\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(t)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}\bigl(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{R_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{R}_{i,\theta}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)-R_{0}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{R_{0}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\,\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{T}_{+}}\widetilde{R}_{i,\theta}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)-R_{0}\bigl((-\infty,t]\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\,\bigl\{\beta q(1-\epsilon)\Phi_{(-2a\gamma,\sigma)}(t)-\Phi_{(0,\sigma)}(t)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\beta q(1-\epsilon)\Phi\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)-\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)=f_{\mathrm{K},b}(a),

where the final inequality follows by choosing
t=-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b}{a\gamma}-a\gamma
. The function
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is continuous as a composition of continuous functions, and the fact that it is strictly increasing follows as in the proof of Lemma
24
, setting
(\epsilon,q)
therein as
(\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.62898pt}{0.43057pt}}{\epsilon},\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q})
, with
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.62898pt}{0.43057pt}}{\epsilon}\coloneqq 1-\beta q(1-\epsilon)
and
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 1.29167pt\hskip 0.58333pt\rule[5.59721pt]{2.80049pt}{0.43057pt}}{q}\coloneqq 1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
14
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
and for the universal constant
C>0
from Lemma
29
, define the event

\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\biggl\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta},R_{n,\theta})\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr\}.

By Lemma
29
, satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\,|\,X_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,X_{n}=x_{n})\geq 1-\delta
, and from now on, we will work on the event
\mathcal{E}
. Recalling that
R_{n,\theta_{0}}\in\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta_{0}},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})\leq d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta_{0}},R_{n,\theta_{0}})\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}.

Moreover, if
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies
d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})>2C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}
, then

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})

\displaystyle\geq d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})-d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta},R_{n,\theta})

\displaystyle>C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\geq d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\widehat{R}_{n,\theta_{0}},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}}),

so
\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}\neq\theta
. Therefore, with
b
and
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
as defined in Lemma
30
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{K}}_{n}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\biggl\{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}:\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})\leq 2C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf\biggl\{a>0:\beta q(1-\epsilon)\Phi\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)-\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\geq 2C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}\biggr\},

(148)

where the second inequality follows since by Lemma
30
,
d_{\mathrm{K}}^{\mathrm{sym}}(R_{n,\theta},\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\mathrm{Lin}})\geq f_{\mathrm{K},b}\bigl(\frac{\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|_{2}}{2}\bigr)
and
f_{\mathrm{K},b}
is a strictly increasing and continuous function. Letting
a=\frac{6\sigma b}{\gamma\sqrt{\log n}}
, we have by our assumption on
b
that
2\sigma b/(a\gamma)-a\gamma/\sigma=\sqrt{\log n}/3-6b/\sqrt{\log n}>0
, so

\displaystyle\beta q(1

\displaystyle-\epsilon)\Phi\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)-\Phi\biggl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}-\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{\beta q(1-\epsilon)}{\bigl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\bigr)+\bigl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\bigr)^{-1}}\cdot\phi\biggl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)-\frac{1}{\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}}\cdot\phi\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\biggl\{\beta q(1-\epsilon)\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}+2b\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}-2b\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle{\geq}\biggl(\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\biggr)^{-1}\frac{3}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\cdot\exp\biggl(-\frac{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{2\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{a^{2}\gamma^{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle{\geq}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log n}}\cdot n^{-5/72}\overset{(v)}{\geq}2C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}}

where
(i)
follows from the Mills ratio bound
\phi(x)/(x+x^{-1})\leq\Phi(-x)\leq\phi(x)/x
for
x>0
;
(ii)
follows since
\frac{1}{2}\leq b\leq\frac{\log n}{36}
implies
\bigl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\bigr)+\bigl(-\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}\bigr)^{-1}\leq\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}+\frac{2\sigma b}{a\gamma}
;
(iii)
follows by substituting the definition of
b
and using the fact that
\beta q(1-\epsilon)\leq 1/2
;
(iv)
follows since
b\leq\frac{\log n}{36}
implies
\frac{a\gamma}{\sigma}\leq\frac{\sigma b}{a\gamma}
; and
(v)
follows from the assumption that
\frac{n^{31/36}}{\log n}\geq C_{1}\bigl\{d+\log(1/\delta)\bigr\}
and taking
C_{1}\coloneqq 4C^{2}
. Therefore, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}_{n}^{\mathrm{K}}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{144\sigma^{2}b^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\log n}\leq\frac{36\sigma^{2}\log^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{4(1-\beta q(1-\epsilon))}{\beta q(1-\epsilon)}\bigr)}{\gamma^{2}\log\bigl(nq(1-\epsilon)\bigr)},

as required.
∎

Appendix F
Auxiliary lemmas

If
\mathcal{Z}
is a topological space, then we define the embedding
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}:C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})^{*}
by
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}(f)(\mu)\coloneqq\mu(f)
. If
\mathcal{Z}
is a locally compact Hausdorff space, then a Borel measure
\mu
on
\mathcal{Z}
is
regular
if
\mu(E)=\inf\{\mu(U):U\supseteq E,U\ \text{open}\}
and
\mu(E)=\sup\{\mu(K):K\subseteq E,K\ \text{compact}\}
for every Borel subset
E
of
\mathcal{Z}
.

Lemma 31
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be a locally compact Hausdorff space in which every open set is
\sigma
-compact. Then
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}
embeds
C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})
into a subspace of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})^{*}
that separates points.

Proof.

If
f,g\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})
and
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, then
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}(\lambda_{1}f+\lambda_{2}g)=\lambda_{1}\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}(f)+\lambda_{2}\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}(g)
, so
\phi_{\mathcal{Z}}
embeds
C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})
into a subspace of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})^{*}
.

Let
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
be two distinct measures in
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})
and define
\nu\coloneqq\mu-\mu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{Z})
. By the the Jordan decomposition theorem
(
Folland 1999
, Theorem 3.3)
, we can write
\nu=\nu_{+}-\nu_{-}
where
\nu_{+},\nu_{-}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathcal{Z})
are supported on disjoint measurable sets
P,N\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
respectively. Since
\nu\neq 0
, there exists a Borel set
B\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
and
\epsilon>0
such that either
\nu_{+}(B\cap P)\geq\epsilon
or
\nu_{-}(B\cap N)\geq\epsilon
. Without loss of generality, we assume the former. By
Folland 1999
,
\nu_{+}
and
\nu_{-}
are regular measures, so there exists a compact set
K\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
and an open set
U\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
such that
K\subseteq B\cap P\subseteq U
and
\nu_{+}(U\setminus K)+\nu_{-}(U\setminus K)\leq\epsilon/2
. By Urysohn’s lemma for locally compact Hausdorff spaces
(
Folland 1999
, Lemma 4.32)
, there exists a continuous function
f:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,1]
such that
f(K)=\{1\}
,
f(U^{c})=\{0\}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\nu(f)

\displaystyle\geq\nu_{+}(K)-\nu_{-}(U\setminus P)\geq\nu_{+}(B\cap P)-\bigl(\nu_{+}(U\setminus K)+\nu_{-}(U\setminus K)\bigr)\geq\epsilon/2.

Consequently,
f\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathcal{Z})
separates
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
as desired.
∎

If
(X,\tau)
and
(Y,\sigma)
are topological spaces, we write
\tau\otimes\sigma
for the product topology on the Cartesian product
X\times Y
, i.e.
\tau\otimes\sigma
is the coarsest topology for which the projections
(x,y)\mapsto x
and
(x,y)\mapsto y
are continuous.

Lemma 32
.

If
X
and
Y
are real vector spaces and
X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
are subspaces of
X^{*}
and
Y^{*}
, then the map
\iota:X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}\rightarrow(X\times Y)^{*}
given by
\iota(f,g)(x,y):=f(x)+g(y)
embeds
X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}
as a subspace of
(X\times Y)^{*}
. Furthermore, if
\tau(X;X^{\prime})
,
\tau(Y;Y^{\prime})
and
\tau\bigl(X\times Y;\iota(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime})\bigr)
denote the weak topologies generated by
X^{\prime}
,
Y^{\prime}
and
\iota(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime})
on
X
,
Y
and
X\times Y
respectively, then
\tau(X;X^{\prime})\otimes\tau(Y;Y^{\prime})=\tau\bigl(X\times Y;\iota(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime})\bigr)
.

Proof.

To check that
\iota
embeds
X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}
as a subspace of
(X\times Y)^{*}
, we only need to verify the bilinearity of the map
((x,y),(f,g))\mapsto f(x)+g(y)
on
(X\times Y)\times(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime})
, which is true since
X,Y,X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}
are vector spaces and
(X,X^{\prime})
,
(Y,Y^{\prime})
are dual pairs.

For the second claim, let
\pi_{X}:X\times Y\to X
and
\pi_{Y}:X\times Y\to Y
be projection maps defined by
\pi_{X}(x,y)\coloneqq x
and
\pi_{Y}(x,y)\coloneqq y
. By the definition of the product topology,
\tau(X;X^{\prime})\otimes\tau(Y;Y^{\prime})
is the coarsest topology on
X\times Y
under which both
\pi_{X}
and
\pi_{Y}
are continuous. Also,
\tau\bigl(X\times Y;\iota(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime})\bigr)
is the coarsest topology on
X\times Y
under which
\iota(f,g)
is continuous for all
f\in X^{\prime}
and
g\in Y^{\prime}
. Hence the desired result is equivalent to the statement that for any topology
\mathcal{T}
on
X\times Y
, the functions
\pi_{X}:(X\times Y,\mathcal{T})\to(X,\tau(X;X^{\prime}))
and
\pi_{Y}:(X\times Y,\mathcal{T})\to(Y,\tau(Y;Y^{\prime}))
are continuous if and only if
\iota(f,g):(X\times Y,\mathcal{T})\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous for all
(f,g)\in X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}
.

The ‘only if’ direction is true since for any
(f,g)\in X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}
,
\iota(f,g)=f\circ\pi_{X}+g\circ\pi_{Y}
is the sum of compositions of continuous functions, and hence continuous. For the ‘if’ direction, we assume that
\iota(f,g)
is continuous for all
(f,g)\in X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime}
; by symmetry we only need to check that
\pi_{X}
is continuous. Taking
g
to be the zero map, we have
\iota(f,0)(x,y)=f(\pi_{X}(x,y))
, so
f\circ\pi_{X}
is continuous for every
f
. Open sets in
(X,\tau(X;X^{\prime}))
are unions of sets in
\{f^{-1}(U):f\in X^{\prime},\text{$U$ open in $\mathbb{R}$}\}
. Since
f\circ\pi_{X}
is continuous, we have

\pi_{X}^{-1}\bigl(f^{-1}(U)\bigr)=(f\circ\pi_{X})^{-1}(U)

is open for every
f\in X^{\prime}
and
U
open in
\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
\pi_{X}
is continuous as desired, and this establishes the lemma.
∎

Lemma 33
.

Let
X,Y,Z
be topological spaces and equip
Y\times Z
with the product topology. Then
f:X\to Y
and
g:X\to Z
are continuous if and only if
h:x\mapsto\bigl(f(x),g(x)\bigr)
is a continuous function from
X
to
Y\times Z
.

Proof.

By definition of the product topology, the projection maps
\pi_{Y}:Y\times Z\to Y
and
\pi_{Z}:Y\times Z\to Z
defined by
\pi_{Y}(y,z)\coloneqq y
and
\pi_{Z}(y,z)\coloneqq z
are continuous. This proves the ‘if’ direction since
f=\pi_{Y}\circ h
and
g=\pi_{Z}\circ h
. For the ‘only if’ direction, we observe that open sets in
Y\times Z
are unions of sets of the form
U\times V
for
U
open in
Y
and
V
open in
Z
. Since
f
and
g
are continuous,
h^{-1}(U\times V)=f^{-1}(U)\cap g^{-1}(V)
is open in
X
, so
h
is continuous as desired.
∎

Recall that if
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are sets, then the
disjoint union
of
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
is defined by
A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}:=\{(a,1):a\in A_{1}\}\cup\{(a,2):a\in A_{2}\}
. Moreover, if
(A_{1},\tau_{1})
and
(A_{2},\tau_{2})
are topological spaces, then
A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
can be endowed with the
disjoint union topology
, given by
\bigl\{(U_{1}\times\{1\})\cup(U_{2}\times\{2\}):U_{1}\in\tau_{1},\,U_{2}\in\tau_{2}\bigr\}
. In the special case where
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are disjoint subsets of a topological space
(\mathcal{X},\tau)
, the second argument of elements in
A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
becomes redundant, so we can identify
